# NCM Online — known bugs

> **STATUS: LEDGER** - living and append-only, current by construction. Covers: *defects (KB-lines)*
> Authority map: [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) - classified 2026-09-24 (L125)

Small, non-blocking defects noted during live testing. Each has an owner side (NCM / platform), a
workaround, and the milestone where it is revisited. Blocking defects are fixed in the work
package that hit them, not listed here.

| # | Seen | What | Side | Workaround | Revisit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| KB-3 | 2026-09-19 | The interaction card's key hint says "E" only; pad players hold X (polled by NCM) but the card cannot show a pad glyph because `open77_interactions` is keyboard-only. The card description says "hold E or X". | platform | text hint | M2.3 |
| KB-4 | 2026-09-20 | **RETESTED 2026-10-04: DOES NOT REPRODUCE.** `o77-client.ps1 -Server 127.0.0.1:11778 -WorldId - -ModsDigest -` (the values the launcher itself passes for dev) gave a second client with 73 WebUI surface lines, 16 imported (log `open77-2026-10-04-08-57-22.log`). Earlier text kept below. **NARROWED 2026-09-26 to the launch method, with two of my own theories refuted on the way. It is not VRAM, not the launch environment, and NOT the startup race I claimed an hour earlier.** `Open77.WebHost.exe` is a separate CEF host process. It connects, then waits for the game to hand it the D3D adapter LUID before starting CEF. Per-instance logs at `plugins/Open77/cache/web/instances/<pid>/open77-webhost.log` show the two outcomes differing by exactly one line -- working: `adapter_pinned_before_cef` then `cef_ready:...:adapter_luid=58981` then `surface_created:N:shared_textures=yes`; failing: **`adapter_unknown_before_cef:starting_unpinned`** then `stop_called` and a dead pipe. **The race theory died when the client was launched ALONE and still failed.** With no other instance running, the orderings are IDENTICAL to a working client -- WebHost connects at +1 s, graphics ready at +5.6 s, pipeline ready -- and the failing one then simply dies 65 ms LATER instead of proceeding. Worse for the theory: that run logged `adapter_pinned_before_cef`, the same line the working client logs. **What actually differs is the next line: the working host reaches `cef_ready:...:adapter_luid=58981`; the failing host gets `stop_called`.** Winsock 995 is `ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED` and `chrome_debug.log` is **0 bytes**, so CEF never started and the host did not crash -- **the game side deliberately tore it down**, and nothing in the game log between pipeline-ready and the disconnect says why. **The correlation is 1:1 across four client logs:** the only one with 0 imported surfaces (48 created) is the only one whose WebHost died; the other three imported 17 each and kept it. Process evidence agrees -- client 1 has an `Open77.WebHost.exe` plus ~24 CEF children alive, client 2 has **none**. **So the variable is isolated to the LAUNCH METHOD, and only that.** Alone-via-launcher renders (every ordinary session); alone-via-`o77-client.ps1` does not. Instance count is out, machine load is out, memory is out (nothing logs an allocation failure anywhere), and the placeholder env vars are out -- they were separately wrong and worth fixing but they are not this. **The single datum still missing is the one I never captured: the launcher-started process's `CommandLine`.** I compared environments and never compared arguments, and the script passes none at all. Capture it while a launcher client is running: `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='Cyberpunk2077.exe'" | Select ProcessId, CommandLine`. There is no timeout or adapter knob: it is compiled into `Open77.dll`/`Open77.WebHost.exe`, so this is a platform limitation NCM cannot patch. **Remaining one-variable tests, cheapest first:** (1) capture and diff the launcher's `CommandLine` against the script's (the script passes no arguments whatsoever); (2) run the script **without** `OP77_IDENTITY_PROFILE`/`OP77_PLAYER_NAME`, which is the only other thing the launcher never sets -- if UI returns, a second identity is the cost, not the launch path; (3) set the working directory to the game root rather than `bind`, since CEF subprocess paths are commonly CWD-relative. **Do not run the first test alone and call it settled:** I have now been wrong twice by explaining one observation with the first mechanism that fit it. **CONSEQUENCE FOR §AZ-B, and it is blocking:** the carrier bridge IS a WebUI surface (`WebUI.create` in `client/carrier_bridge.lua`), so a client with no WebHost has no bridge, no Parts and no telemetry no matter what `KB-147` did to the ports. **§AZ-B cannot run until a second client keeps its WebHost.** Superseded framing kept for the record: the row previously blamed VRAM, then the launch environment. **Original measurement, which was right all along:** The owner asked why a second client cannot render a UI when that was asserted on day one; it does not survive the question. **(a) The row contradicts itself:** it says *none is ever imported/rendered (0)* and then says *the venue prompt card did render*. Both cannot be true, so `imported` and `rendered` are not the same event and the count measured only the first. **(b) The attributed cause was never measured.** *"Consistent with the M0 note that a second in-world client is VRAM-limited"* is a hypothesis with no VRAM figure recorded, no test at reduced settings, and no attempt to separate it from the launch method. **(c) The launch method was demonstrably wrong.** `tools/o77-client.ps1` passed `OP77_WORLD_SERVER=-` and `OP77_WORLD_MODS_DIGEST=-`. The launcher passes a real world GUID and the server's real required-mods digest on **every** launch -- verified in `launcher.log` and against `launcher-settings.json`, where the launcher keys its whole mod profile on `activeProfile: "world:<guid>"`. A client told `-` for both is announcing that it is joining no known world with no known mod stack, and every KB-4 observation was taken from a client launched that way. **Fixed 2026-09-26:** the script now resolves the world id from launcher settings, the endpoint from the launcher's last successful launch, and the digest live from the public manifest (it changed twice on 2026-09-26 alone, so hard-coding it guarantees staleness); it refuses to launch with placeholders unless `-SkipDigest` is passed for an explicit A/B. It also **counts imported WebUI surfaces automatically after launch**, so the original measurement is reproduced by running it rather than argued about. **NOT YET RETESTED** -- the fix is a corrected experiment, not a result. Note `KB-147`: even with UI working, a second instance's carrier cannot bind its port, so §AZ-B needs both. One thing the original row got right and is worth keeping: CEF cache is already per-instance (`plugins/Open77/cache/web/instances/<pid>/`), so a browser-singleton collision was never the explanation. a second game instance creates every WebUI surface but none is ever imported/rendered (client 1 log: 18 `WebUI surface … imported`; client 2: 0). The venue prompt card did render on that instance (so at least the interactions surface draws), but chat/notifications did not, and **key input does not reach interactions** (hold E at the prompt does nothing). Lua logic (calendar, markers, gates, GPS) runs normally. Consistent with the M0 note that a second in-world client is VRAM-limited. | platform / hardware | client 2: issue a car from the server console (`ncm.car <pid> sport`), enter via dev console `resource.emit ncm:venuePrompt`, other commands via `resource.emit ncm:console …`; read its state from the server log | revisit if a second machine or a lighter second client becomes available |

| KB-7 | 2026-09-20 | A server with a loopback or private-LAN `publicEndpoint`/`publicBaseUrl` registers with the master (lease granted) but gets **no catalogue row**: not listed, deep link resolves to a raw address, required mods never reach the launcher (Warden: "IP must be routable for public listing"). | platform (by design) | develop locally without hosted mods; prove E13 on the routable staging server (M7-lite.0) | ask devs whether unlisted rows exist for private endpoints |
| KB-8 | 2026-09-20 | **Hosted required mods are non-functional on the alpha master.** On the routable staging server the catalogue row appears (KB-7 confirmed) and the launcher lists our packages ("4 packages, 2 executable, 46.2 MB"), but every Play is refused in ~300 ms from the master's verdicts without touching our server: hosted packages come back `Unverifiable` (undocumented; docs say unreviewed = `unknown` → consent gate), indexed `archivexl`/`tweakxl` come back `NotInIndex` although the docs list them as indexed loaders (the signed stack index, stable gen 58, has 6 packages, no loaders). Proven exhaustively: secured → refused; indexed loaders → `NotInIndex`; `requiredMods.unsecured: true` with the loader zips hosted → launcher logs `capability cap lifted for unsecured world` and shows the UNSECURED consent dialog, then still refuses all four as `Unverifiable`. Zero `GET /mods/v1/manifest` ever reached the server. **RE-TESTED AND SHARPENED 2026-09-22 on .87, with the owner's Discord observation as the prompt ("someone asked why connecting says unsecured; devs said it is about mods, and the stress test ran with mods") — i.e. hosted mods demonstrably work for somebody, so the fault is likelier ours than the platform's.** Five findings, in order of how much they change things:

**(1) Every KB-8 test before today was invalid.** All of them declared **four** packages, but reviews were only ever queued for our two (E13). ArchiveXL and TweakXL are third-party loaders we self-host as blobs and never submitted, so one refused package failed the whole join and the result said nothing about our own mods. **`set-unsecured.py` was written to drop exactly those two and silently never did** — its regex assumes one-line entries (`\{ "id": "archivexl", "minVersion": "..." \},`) while `server.jsonc` writes multi-line objects, so `re.sub` matched nothing and the script printed success regardless. New `tools/staging/drop-loader-mods.py` removes brace-balanced objects properly.

**(2) With only our two reviewed packages declared, both are still `Unverifiable`.** Launcher log, 2026-09-22: `connect refused: 'NCM Staging' requires mods that cannot be installed ncm-rb15-liveries: Unverifiable; rb15-f1-inert-v10: Unverifiable`. So the reviews have not landed — finally a clean answer rather than an artefact of the loaders.

**(3) The transport was never the problem.** `http://52.60.225.137:11779/mods/v1/manifest` answers **HTTP 200** publicly with the correct digest and sha256s. And during the attempt **nothing whatsoever reached the server** — no `RESOURCE HTTP`, no connect ticket, no connection. The refusal is entirely launcher↔master.

**(4) `Unverifiable` is NOT the same state as `unreviewed`, and the launcher's own message says so by contradiction.** It logs `capability cap lifted for unsecured world 'NCM Staging': unreviewed executable packages will be accepted after the player's consent` and then refuses anyway. Either `Unverifiable` means "the master has no record of this package at all" (which would imply **our review requests never actually registered** — they were made via a browser-console `fetch(.../request-review)`, which could have failed silently), or the unsecured consent path does not cover it and the message is misleading. **CORRECTED same day by the Warden Mods page: the reviews DID register.** Both packages show `review requested Sep 20 14:08:39`, so the console POST worked and nothing is broken on our side. That disproves the "never registered" half and leaves the other: **`Unverifiable` is what an awaiting-review hosted package returns, and the unsecured consent path does not cover it** — so the launcher's own message ("unreviewed executable packages will be accepted after the player's consent") is either wrong or describes a different state. Warden shows *that* a review was requested but **no status for it** — no pending/approved/rejected — so from our side a queued review and an ignored one are indistinguishable. **That is the question for the devs**, and it is now a specific one: two INERT hosted packages, reviews requested 2026-09-20 14:08 UTC, still `Unverifiable` 2 days later.

**(5) The signed stack index still has no loaders, and the client has moved ahead of the server.** `modstack: index verified — channel stable, generation 61, 6 package(s)` (KB-8 originally recorded gen 58, also 6). Contents are `open77-core`, `open77-webui`, `red4ext`, `redscript`, `cybercmd-compat` — **no ArchiveXL/TweakXL**, so "indexed loaders" is not available to us and hosting them is the only route. Separately, `modstore/state/installed.json` shows the client running **`open77-core 2.31.13+op77.88`** while **our server is .87** — a version gap worth closing before drawing further conclusions. | platform (alpha master behind its docs) | staging parked at `requiredMods.enabled=false, unsecured=false` (packages stay imported: rb15-f1-inert-v10, ncm-rb15-liveries, archivexl 1.27.1, tweakxl 1.11.4; `tools/staging/set-unsecured.py` / `set-mods-off.py` flip the flags — note Warden imports rewrite the block and drop `unsecured`). Review requested for both NCM packages via `POST /api/mods/{id}/request-review` (200, queued 2026-09-20). TW1 fallback = manual install per tester + launcher foreign-file allow (E13 stays open). | Discord question to devs (drafted in wp/M7-lite.0): meaning of `Unverifiable`, loaders `NotInIndex`, review turnaround |

| KB-23 | 2026-09-21 | **Race time came back as exactly `0:40.000`.** Same session's qualifying produced `1:04.964`, so the timer has real sub-second precision — a race result landing on a perfectly round 40.000 is suspicious. GREEN was logged at `1790013034.082` and the session completed ~40 s later, which suggests the race time may be taken from the phase-change tick rather than from the finish-gate acceptance sample. Worth confirming before any record or championship depends on it. | NCM (`session/race.lua` finish resolver / `ncm_league/server/race.lua`) | compare `session.green_at` against the gate acceptance timestamp in SQL | **DIAGNOSED 2026-09-22 from the first two-human race.** Not "always .000" — **race times are quantised to the 100 ms sampling tick**. Evidence: solo run `0:40.000` = 400×100 ms; two-human race `6:37.200` = 3972×100 ms; but qualifying returns `1:04.964`, which is **not** a multiple of 100 ms. So quali interpolates the crossing and the race path snaps to the sample that happened to observe it. Two different precisions for the same act of crossing a line. **Fix: interpolate the race gate crossing the way quali already does** — the swept-segment sign flip gives the exact fraction between samples. Must land before records mean anything. **FIXED 2026-09-21 (unverified live) — and there were two causes, not one, both of the form "we recorded when we noticed, not when it happened".** (a) **Green was dated by the tick that observed it.** `session/race.lua` set `s.greenAt = now` inside `step()`, which runs at `raceTickMs` (1000 ms), so green was stamped up to a full second after `startsAt` and every race time carried that inflation. It is a *shared* offset, so gaps between drivers stayed correct — which is exactly why nobody caught it. Now `s.greenAt = s.startsAt`: the lights go out when they were scheduled to, and the tick merely notices. (b) **A stream crossing was dated by the packet that carried it.** `telemetry.lua` ingest passed `t` (the batch's arrival time) to `streamGate` — one value shared by all five samples in the batch, up to a second after the car actually crossed. New `course.gates.crossFraction(g, prev, pos)` returns where in the swept segment the plane was cut, so the host computes `at = a.t + (b.t - a.t) * f`. The `captureM` / `leaveM` rules have no sign flip and honestly return 1 rather than inventing precision. Regression test `kb23_crossings_and_green_are_dated_not_sampled`. **Live QA: a race time on the same route should now look like a quali time — not a multiple of 100 ms.**

Harmless today, but it makes "which build is on staging?" ambiguous, which is exactly the question asked when something breaks mid-tester-window. | NCM (manifest vs banner string) | trust `/api/resources` | **FIXED 2026-09-21 (unverified live).** `WORLD_VERSION` synced to the manifest's `0.5.0` with a comment to keep them in step.

This row's original side-attribution ("NCM (no class ceiling applied)") was exactly right. Now: `ncx_rb15` = **355**, new `class.recordCeilings()` expresses the ceiling per vehicle *record* (the shape the governor actually takes), `NcmCompetition.publishCeilings()` writes them to `GlobalState.ncmCeilings`, and each client applies them for itself. The API pair is probed before use, never half: a half-present governor could set a cap it could not clear. Two tests, one asserting the ceiling never rises above 355. |

| KB-34 | 2026-09-21 | **REVISED AGAIN 2026-09-22, and the real defect is now clear: CAR-TO-CAR CONTACT IS BEING RECORDED AS SCENERY CONTACT.** In the first two-human race the other driver **flipped the owner's car over a barrier**, yet all seven incidents logged `kind=object` with **`other=nil`** — not one was classified car-to-car. The detector, bands, escalation and SR arithmetic are all proven correct (owner bands 3+3+2 = −0.80; visitor 2+1+3+3 = −0.90), and core's `test_incidents.car_car_is_symmetric_and_deduped` passes, **so the rules engine supports car-to-car and the host is simply never supplying the counterpart**. Likely cause: the host evaluates each car's own motion/damage in isolation and never checks for another canonical vehicle at the moment of impact. Separately, the solo time trial produced no incidents at all, which may be the same gap seen from the other side (no neighbour → no evaluation path). | NCM (`competition/incidents` + the host's detection inputs) | none | **fix pass, highest priority of the incident work.** Attribution is not cosmetic: **Sportsman, Rival, Chaos Agent, fault attribution and the whole social layer of the Fingerprint depend on knowing WHO hit WHOM**, and under L74c that history is permanent. Everything logged so far says nobody has ever touched anybody, which is false. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-21 (unverified live).** The host was never at fault for *not calling* `carCar` — it calls it every tick. It was feeding it **samples taken at different moments**. `NcmTelemetry.scan` compared each driver's `ring:last(1)` against every other driver's `ring:last(1)`; those come from independent clients sampling at 5 Hz, accepted up to a **3 s** freshness tolerance apart. At 50 m/s even 0.2 s of skew is 10 m — more than twice `contactM` (4.5 m) — so two cars genuinely touching almost never read as in contact. **The same skew broke the other half:** `Incidents.object`'s "nobody within `neighbourM` (8 m)" test cleared just as wrongly, so the contact fell through and was filed `kind=object, other=nil`. One cause, both symptoms, exactly as observed. Also: the scan ran at 1 Hz over 5 Hz data, so 80% of samples were never examined. **Fix, three parts:** (a) `telemetry.sampleAt(samples, t)` — linear interpolation onto a common instant, returning nil outside the observed span (never extrapolates); (b) `scan` now walks a common clock at 0.2 s steps across the interval since the last scan and places every driver on it, sorted for determinism; (c) core refuses instead of guessing — `carCar` returns `nil, "unaligned"` beyond `alignSec` (0.25 s), and `object` returns `nil, "neighbour_unknown"` when a neighbour is stale or untimed, because an unplaceable car is not an absent car. Three regression tests (`kb34_*`). **Live QA: two humans, deliberate contact — expect `kind=car` with `other` populated on both, and the SR drop on both.** |

| KB-37 | 2026-09-22 | **A driver in a live session is auto-entered into the next scheduled event and then DNS'd.** While session `9dba7550` was still running, the next cadence event formed at 01:19:01 with `entrants=2` — the same two drivers — and immediately completed `no_starters`, classifying both as **DNS** (`P1 A DNS | P2 baremetaleven DNS`). Harmless today (+0 accomplishments) but it writes false DNS history, and DNS feeds participation and reliability records. | NCM (`ncm_league/server/race.lua` form path) | none | **FIXED 2026-09-21 (unverified live), and the owner's hypothesis is supported.** There is no auto-enter mechanism — `entrantsOf` only accepts `checked_in`, and the only writers of that state are `ncm:enter` / `ncm.enter` / `scenario.lua`. So the entries were made deliberately, and `ncm.race.now` is the likely trigger: it sets `startsAt = t + 180` on the next eligible event, whose 3-minute fuse then burns while its entrants are out in a different race. **The defect is the missing guard, which holds however the entries arose.** New `NcmRace.busySession(driverId)` returns the unfinished session holding a driver (entrant state `entered/ready/racing/suspended`). Used in two places: `entrantsOf` leaves busy drivers out of the field — so the event forms with nobody and `formSession` returns nil, rather than forming and DNS'ing them, because their entry stays `checked_in` and **unjudged** (a scheduling collision is not a sporting fact); and `enterPlayer` now refuses a second entry outright. Held-back entrants are logged once per event, not once per tick. **Unconfirmed:** how those two entries were created — needs `SELECT * FROM entry WHERE event_id IN (...)` for the two events next time we are on the box. |
| KB-38 | 2026-09-22 | **+2 accomplishments granted on a race with three incidents.** `driver=72519ab3 sr 4.60 -> 3.80 … accomplishments +2` — SR fell 0.80 from three object incidents, so the run was emphatically not clean, yet two accomplishments were granted. `first_finish` and `first_win` are `once = true` and this driver already held both, and `clean_race` requires zero incidents, so it is unclear what the two are. Needs `/ncm.profile` and an `accomplishment` table read to identify them. | NCM (`core/driver/progression.lua` or the league's save path) | — | **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-21 (unverified live).** `grant(kind, refId, once)` guarded with `if has(state, kind, once and nil or refId)`. In Lua `once and nil or refId` **always evaluates to `refId`** — `true and nil` is `nil`, which is falsy, so `or refId` wins; and `false and nil` is `false`, so `or refId` wins too. The `once` flag was inert. "Have I ever earned this?" was therefore asked as "have I earned this in **this** session?", which no earlier session can satisfy — so `first_finish`, `first_win` and `first_time_trial` were re-granted **every race**. The two on a three-incident race were `first_finish` + `first_win`: the driver finished and was P1, and `clean_race` correctly declined. **The duplicates are real DB rows:** `accomplishment`'s unique key is `(ncm_driver_id, server_id, kind, ref_id)` and `ref_id` is the session id, so `INSERT IGNORE` never caught them — which is also why `/ncm.profile` read `first_finish×2, first_win×2` (KB/master-test §1.5). Fixed with an explicit branch; regression test `kb38_once_only_accomplishments_are_granted_once`. **Outstanding: existing rows need deduping** — see the cleanup note below; not run, it deletes rows. |
| KB-39 | 2026-09-22 | **A visitor can sit for minutes never becoming gameplay-ready, then leave.** The platform warned for player 3 at 81 s and player 4 at **260 s**: *"still cannot confirm they are in the world … means no resource is emitting `open77:session:gameplayReady`"*. A first-time visitor's whole experience can therefore be four minutes of nothing. | **platform** (NCM neither emits nor can emit this) | reconnect | **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-21; NCM-side mitigation FIXED (unverified live), the cause is the platform's to fix.** `open77:session:gameplayReady` is emitted by the platform's own appearance shell — `open77_appearance/client/main.lua`, `announce()` — which opens with `if announced or failed ~= nil then return end`. **Once an appearance restore has failed, the readiness signal is never sent again for that session: there is no retry path.** That is precisely why reconnecting works and waiting does not, and it matches the M3.1 note (`stage=appearance_failed`, "Reconnect to retry") which was filed at the time as unrelated to NCM — correctly, but it was the same defect. **NCM must not emit the signal itself:** it would tell freeroam to run a join spawn for a player whose world is not up. Note also that `onPlayerReady` is *not* this signal — it is the hold barrier lifting, which says only that nobody is blocking the player, and the platform's own comment says as much. **Mitigation shipped:** `ncm_world/server/main.lua` watches every ready player who never acquires a fresh position, and at 45 s (then every 60 s, giving up at 300 s) tells them in chat and a toast to reconnect, and logs a host-facing line naming KB-39. Four minutes of silence becomes an actionable instruction in forty-five seconds. **Report upstream:** the `failed ~= nil` latch in `announce()` needs a retry or a recovery path. |

| KB-49 | 2026-09-22 | **Core tests share one mutable catalogue, so a test can pass or fail depending on what ran before it.** `data.nodes.LIST` and `data.roots.LIST` are module-level tables with `register`/`unregister`, and `tests/run.lua` uses **one Core for every test file**. Any test that registers a node leaves it behind for every later file, in alphabetical order. Found while writing `test_roots`: an invariant of the form "every node has a name and a region" failed on `c_ring_s`, then on `c_p_s` — neither a curated node, both left over from `test_competition`. The invariant was true of the authored network and false of the suite's accumulated state. | NCM (test runner has no per-file isolation; catalogues are shared mutable state by design) | write invariants against a filtered subset rather than the whole LIST | **Recorded, not fixed.** Two candidate fixes, neither free: (a) snapshot and restore `LIST` around each test file in `run.lua`, which changes a PROVIDED file every brick also ships; (b) require registered test fixtures to carry `custom = true` and have invariants ignore them, which is a convention nothing enforces. **The live risk is not the tests** — it is that `Roots.register` mutates shared state the same way at runtime, which is exactly why the ephemeral-route design (IDEA-106) needs a janitor. |
| KB-50 | 2026-09-22 | **PLATFORM: vehicle sync degrades when many vehicles are concentrated in a small area — which is the exact shape of a race grid.** From the OPEN//77 stress-test report (Discord, 2026-09-22): 40+ players at peak, **no server crashes**, PvP and large-scale FFA smooth, server stable under deliberate abuse. The one substantial finding is **vehicle synchronisation with a large number of vehicles in the same area**, with client FPS and client-side sync workload named as the cost. The devs' own guidance: *"if you're planning a gamemode with a large number of players and vehicles concentrated in a very small area, you should still be careful with player density for now."* **NCM is that gamemode.** A 20-car grid at current geometry is 10 rows × `ROW_SPACING_M` 7.0 m = a **70 m × 5.2 m box containing 20 networked vehicles**, held stationary for a countdown, then accelerating together — the densest thing anyone can do on this platform. L60's TW2 ambition is exactly this case. **We have never run more than two cars.** | platform (sync protocol, actively being worked on) + NCM (density is ours to choose) | none needed yet — nothing we run today is dense | **Not a blocker, a design constraint to hold consciously.** Knobs NCM already owns, none of which need new systems: `session.grid` `ROW_SPACING_M` / `COLUMN_OFFSET_M` / `FIRST_ROW_BEHIND_M`; per-championship `capacity` (20 today); grid-car spawning **already staggered at 150 ms per car**; venue dressing props and spectator NPCs, which add entity count at the same moment and place; and **rolling starts, which spread the field over ~300 m of formation and are therefore *less* dense than a standing grid** — worth remembering when KB-46's missing ring route gets authored. **Also: OPEN//77 enters OPEN BETA by the end of this week**, which changes who can arrive (KB-36, and the whitelist now in force). The devs say they will publish the server-side stress reports — worth reading before TW2 sizing. **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-22: wait for open beta before acting.** *"they said open beta by end of the week and highlighted this as their issue, in fact gives me optimism that they are working on it now so im inclined to give it time. we are not on a deadline. check in at beta."* So IDEA-122's density measurement is **deferred to the beta check-in**, not dropped — measuring against a build they are actively fixing would produce numbers with a short shelf life. **UPDATE 2026-09-24 -- the platform says this is fixed.** OPEN//77 devs, with video: *"here is more then 200 vehicles synced in multiplayer, working perfectly... imagine the potential with the servers! even in FiveM you cannot do this."* Posted directly at the stress-test finding above, three days later. **CORRECTION, same day:** I first hedged that 200 vehicles across a city would not measure density per area, which is what this finding actually named. **The owner watched the video: it is a dense ring of cars circulating in a small area** -- the exact shape KB-50 warned about, not a citywide scatter. So the finding is superseded properly and not merely improved upon. **What remains untested is ours, not the platform's:** that footage is the devs' own build with no NCM resources, no race HUD and no NCM client workload, and circulating traffic is not twenty player-driven cars sitting in a standing grid at lights-out. `IDEA-122` therefore survives as **sizing**, with no expectation of finding a wall. |

## 2026-09-22 status addenda

- **KB-36:** staging now has an active whitelist. Live evidence at 15:28 UTC refused `[OP//77 Team] Yuki`
  with `This server is private … not on its whitelist.` The discovery question remains historically useful, but
  unsolicited visitors are no longer the current staging posture. Every TW1 tester must be added before joining.
- **KB-43 / empty Race Guide:** deploy 11 diagnosis proved the server builds and writes 16 Guide rows successfully
  (`guide published player=2 rows=16`) while F6 remains on its cached empty value. Fixed locally for the next deploy:
  `pushGuide` now sends a direct `ncm:guide.payload` response as well as retaining the player-bag mirror, and the
  client logs receipt. **Validated live on deploy 12:** the Guide populated, interest and leaving updated correctly,
  and My Races worked. P1–P2 PASS; KB-43 is closed.
| KB-51 | 2026-09-22 | **An OPEN//77 upgrade can silently take NCM offline: `resources.load` is an allow-list, and a new platform resource that an existing one depends on is never loaded.** Hit on .84 -> .87. .87 adds `open77_cyberware_lab` and makes the shipped `freeroam` gamemode depend on it (`gamemodes/freeroam/open77.lua:9`). No .84-era config could have listed a resource that did not exist, so the lab was never loaded and the server logged `[ERR] Automatic resource start failed: Resource 'open77_cyberware_lab' was not found.` **That aborts the WHOLE automatic start set.** The server still came up, listened on 11778, discovered 41 resources and registered with the master — so every surface-level health check passed — while `ncm_world` and `ncm_league` were simply absent for about an hour. **The misleading part is that the failure names a platform resource NCM does not use**, which sends you looking at packaging and discovery rather than at the load list. | platform (upgrade adds a dependency the operator's config cannot know about) + ops (allow-list must be reconciled per upgrade) | none — NCM is down until it is fixed | **FIXED 2026-09-22 and guarded.** Fix was one config line: `open77_cyberware_lab` added to `resources.load` after `open77_reflex` (its three dependencies — equipment, dash, reflex — all appear earlier), via `tools/staging/add-cyberware-lab.py`. **The package-shipped `gamemodes/freeroam/open77.lua` was deliberately NOT edited**: removing the dependency there also works but silently conflicts on every future upgrade. **Guard: `tools/staging/check-load-list.py`** — cross-checks every declared dependency of every loaded resource against `resources.load` and exits non-zero if any is missing. **Run it after every upgrade, before restarting.** Verified clean on .87: `load list OK: 38 resources, every dependency satisfied`. Note manifests use single *or* double quotes (`open77_animations`, `open77_player_interactions` use single) — the guard handles both; an earlier version reported false positives because it did not. |
| KB-52 | 2026-09-22 | **The .87 upgrade silently cleared the whitelist.** Enabled 2026-09-22 01:52 UTC (`access.whitelist … enabled=True: 1 identity may connect`) and enforcing correctly at 15:28, when `[OP//77 Team] Yuki` was refused by name. After the ~17:30 upgrade it was gone: `ncmtester2` (`d82ed6cf…`) connected at 19:09 and a stranger, `Dimas` (`f48b39c8…`), at 19:12. **The clincher is not the stranger but our own test client** — ncmtester2 had never connected since the whitelist went up, so it was never one of the allowed identities, and it walked in. **Warden's audit log holds exactly one whitelist entry ever** (the enable), and no disable or edit — an operator action would have been audited, so this was state loss, not a change. `acl.jsonc` (Sep 20) and the whole `.open77/` tree survived the upgrade intact, so the whitelist lives somewhere else that the upgrade replaced. | platform (upgrade does not preserve access state) + ops | re-enable in Warden after every upgrade | **Recorded, not fixed — and deliberately deprioritised.** Owner, 2026-09-22: *"honestly i dont care. if they see plumbing, they see plumbing."* Access is a courtesy problem here, not a security one (L90). **The real lesson is the pattern:** this is the *second* thing the .87 upgrade silently broke, after `open77_cyberware_lab` took NCM offline for an hour (KB-51). Both were invisible until something went wrong. **Add a whitelist check to the post-upgrade steps** beside `check-load-list.py`. Identity to re-add alongside the owner's: ncmtester2 `d82ed6cf-c5cb-4dd8-8473-3a1d0057d3b7`. |
| KB-53 | 2026-09-22 | **The Vehicle Collection could never discover anything: `Open77.vehicles.getPosition` does not exist.** The polling loop read position with `pcall(Open77.vehicles.getPosition, liveId)`. That function is not part of the platform — `Open77.vehicles.get(id)` returns a snapshot carrying **flat** `x`/`y`/`z` (this is exactly how the platform's own `/vehicle.list` prints a vehicle), no OPEN//77 Lua resource anywhere calls `getPosition`, and the only match in `Open77.Server.Core.dll` is the C# property getter `get_Position`. So `pcall` called nil, returned `false, "attempt to call a nil value"`, set `same = false`, set `dist = math.huge`, and failed the radius test **every time, for every vehicle, forever** — with no log line, because the pcall swallowed it. **Worse, the diagnostic disagreed with the live path:** `ncm.collection.probe` used the *correct* `vehicles.get` with a flat-coordinate fallback, so it would have printed `verdict=candidate` for encounters that could never be awarded. | NCM (unverified platform API) | none — the feature was inert | **FIXED 2026-09-22, pre-deploy, never shipped.** One `snapshot(liveId)` helper now resolves record, bucket and position, and **both** the loop and the probe call it. The probe reports why it rejected (`no_snapshot` / `record_mismatch` / `bucket` / `distance`) instead of a bare `reject`. **The pattern, sixth instance this week:** correct-looking code that nothing successfully invoked (`performanceFor`, `Class.eligible`, `route.description`, `set-unsecured.py`'s regex, the weather service failing behind log-and-continue, now this). Five of the six were silent. **`pcall` around an API you have not verified exists converts "this platform has no such function" into "this never matched".** Verify the symbol before wrapping the call. |
| KB-54 | 2026-09-22 | **A failed discovery read was cached as "discovered nothing".** `load(driverId)` ended `... or {}` on the query and cached the result, so an unreachable or failed read cached **an empty collection** for that driver for the rest of the process lifetime. Two consequences: their Collection panel reported `0 discovered`, and the next encounter with a car they had already found was treated as a first discovery — firing `NEW VEHICLE DISCOVERED` for a car they had owned for days. | NCM | none | **FIXED 2026-09-22, pre-deploy.** Same shape as the ownership defect settled earlier: **a failed read must return nil, not an empty answer.** Absent and empty are different facts and only one of them is safe to cache. `load` returns nil, does not cache, and logs loudly; `payload` refuses with `collection_unavailable`; `recordEncounter` still writes the encounter (the SQL is idempotent) but stays silent rather than announcing a discovery it cannot verify is new. |
| KB-55 | 2026-09-22 | **A half-written catalogue generation bricked the Collection permanently.** `NcmCollection.start()` published generation membership as N separate un-`IGNORE`d inserts with no verification. If that loop died partway — one DB hiccup — the next boot saw a non-empty but short membership, failed `#members ~= count()`, and returned `catalogue_generation_changed_without_bump` **on every subsequent boot**. The reason was also a lie: nothing had changed, the write had been interrupted. Only manual SQL could recover it. | NCM | manual SQL | **FIXED 2026-09-22, pre-deploy.** A bare row count genuinely cannot tell a partial write from a changed catalogue — but the generation row's `vehicle_count` can, and it was **written and then never read**. It is the *declared* size: `declared == catalogue count` with rows missing means an interrupted publish, so finish it with `INSERT IGNORE`; `declared ~= catalogue count` means the catalogue really did change under a published generation, so refuse and print **both** numbers so the operator knows whether to bump. Membership is now re-read and verified after writing, rather than assumed from the fact that the inserts returned. |
| KB-56 | 2026-09-22 | **One unnamed car would have taken down the entire league service.** `data.vehicle_catalogue` built its index with `assert(META[lookup], …)` inside the module body. That module is a `shared_script`, and `server/collection.lua` calls `NcmCore.get("data.vehicle_catalogue")` at **file scope** — so a pool record without a display name raises at resource load and takes `ncm_league` with it: calendar, races, settlement, standings, weather, all of it, because one car lacked a manufacturer string. Adding a vehicle to the pool is an ordinary authoring act and must not be able to do that. `addRuntime` also silently rebound a record claimed by two machines (last write won while the record stayed listed under the first). | NCM | none — total outage | **FIXED 2026-09-22, pre-deploy.** Authoring faults are now **collected** into `M.unmapped()` and `M.conflicts()` rather than raised, moving the blast radius to where it belongs: the **offline gate** asserts both lists are empty, so the fault fails loudly where it costs nothing to fix; `NcmCollection.start()` refuses to start *itself* on a non-empty list — an unnameable machine must never be frozen into an immutable generation — leaving the rest of the league service untouched; and `ncm.collection.probe` prints the lists instead of a stack trace. Duplicate `vehicleKey`s are recorded as conflicts too. |
| KB-57 | 2026-09-22 | **The Vehicle Collection's tests could not fail.** Two of the three shipped tests proved nothing. `T.eq(cat.count(), #pool.allRecords())` was true **by construction** — the catalogue was built by iterating `allRecords()` and appending one entry per record, so the assertion restated the loop. The "append-only" test called `M.countWith(additions)`, a helper that existed **only for that test**, whose entire body was `#entries + #(additions or {})`; it asserted `n + 1 == n + 1`, exercised no catalogue behaviour, and had no other caller. | NCM | none | **FIXED 2026-09-22, pre-deploy.** Replaced with eight tests covering what can actually go wrong: unmapped records, duplicate `vehicleKey`s, unreachable entries, missing display metadata, livery/`_player` collapse, non-string and live-id inputs to `resolveRecord`, stable headline category for a multi-class machine, and slot uniqueness/stability. **Verified by mutation** — deleting one `META` row turns 138/0 into 136/2. A test that cannot fail is worse than no test: it spends the gate's credibility without buying anything. |
| KB-58 | 2026-09-22 | **Players can arrive inside each other, and both puppets stick.** Seen when a visitor spawned directly into player 1. Cyberpunk resolves two puppets at one point by refusing to resolve it: neither can walk out, and only a teleport frees them. Three NCM-visible paths produce it — a plain **join**, because the platform's spawn point does not know who is already standing on it; NCM's own **resume teleport**, which deliberately returns a dropped racer to their last position, a position someone else may since have walked into; and **`ncm.tp`**, whose entire job is to put one player beside another. | platform (spawn point is not occupancy-aware) + NCM (resume and tp aim at remembered points) | `ncm.tp <pid> <otherPid>` to shove them somewhere else | **FIXED offline 2026-09-22, awaiting deploy 14.** NCM cannot choose the platform's spawn point, so the guard runs *after* arrival: if a player is within **3 m** of another in the same bucket, walk a ring of 8 offsets and teleport them to the first clear one — small, sideways, same ground, **no fade** (fading the screen for a half-step reads as a bug). Applied on join, on resume and after `ncm.tp`. The join check waits for `inTheWorld` first, because `onPlayerReady` fires before the puppet exists (KB-39) and separating immediately would measure a position that is not there yet. Manual escape hatch: **`ncm.unstick <playerId>`**. If every offset is occupied it logs and leaves them alone rather than flinging anyone. |
| KB-59 | 2026-09-22 | **The Race Guide payload stopped reaching clients after deploy 12: the information layer made it too big for the net event.** Symptom: F6 sits on *"Loading live schedule…"* forever and the clock reads **31 Dec 1969**. Both are one cause — the panel opens with `lastGuide or { rows = {}, loading = true }`, that placeholder has no `serverNow`, so `guideOffset` stays `null`, `guideNow()` returns `0`, and `new Date(0)` renders as 1969 west of UTC. For the placeholder to persist, **no payload ever arrived**. **Evidence is decisive:** the client log at 15:38–15:44 (deploy 11) shows `guide payload received: 16 row(s)` every 10 s; the live session from 20:11 onward has **zero** such lines, while `[ncm_world/client/calendar] calendar: 3 upcoming` keeps arriving — so GlobalState still works and only the large net-event payload is lost. Row count is not the trigger: the old session went 16 → 17 rows and kept working. | NCM (mine — deploy 12 information layer) | none; the Guide is unusable | **FIXED — deploy 18, owner-confirmed rendering.** Took four stacked defects: this one (oversized net payload), KB-61 (focus soft-lock), KB-63 (silent WebUI message drop) and the script-budget ceiling inside KB-63's first fix. Each was necessary, none sufficient, and each hid the next. **Original note follows.** Deploy 14 evidence: `guide published player=1 rows=17 bytes=17948` server-side and `guide payload received: 17 row(s)` client-side at 20:38:03 — the first such line since deploy 11. **So the payload now arrives and the Guide is still blank: there is a SECOND defect underneath, which the transport failure was hiding.** Note 17,948 bytes did not trip the 24 KB warning, so the size theory is unconfirmed — the trim fixed it, but whether the old payload failed on bytes or on an unserializable value in `recent` is still unknown. Cause of the first defect: Cause: `weatherBlock()` put `NcmWeather.readModel()` into the payload **whole** — current conditions, every forecast band in a 24 h horizon **and** the last sixteen observations — after 11_WEATHER.md had specified "one bounded read model". Fix: `weatherForClient()` sends only current conditions and provenance; the full model stays server-side for `eventWeather()`, which was the only thing that needed it. `recent` is dropped outright — nothing renders it, and its `preset`/`reason` come straight off a DB row without `tostring`/`tonumber`, the one part of the payload whose types we do not control. **Also instrumented:** every publish now JSON-encodes the payload and logs `bytes=`, with `GUIDE PAYLOAD LARGE` above 24 KB and `GUIDE PAYLOAD NOT ENCODABLE` if it cannot be encoded at all. Deploy 11 already proved a bag write can succeed without arriving; a payload that silently fails to arrive is indistinguishable from an empty Guide, and a number in the log turns that from a guess into a fact. |
| KB-60 | 2026-09-22 | **The KB-39 limbo watchdog never runs for the players who need it most: it hangs off `onPlayerReady`, which never fires in the failure mode it was written for.** First in-the-wild bug report. A visitor (`Trip[ABK]`, player 6) connected, appeared in chat, and was visible to `ncm.where` **with a position** — but the platform logged `stage=appearance_failed` **44 times** over ~11 minutes and NCM never logged `ready: player=6`. Because `watchForLimbo` is started from `onPlayerReady`, it never started: no warning, no reconnect advice, no welcome, no car offer. They said *"wtf is this appearance is still settling?"*, *"cant join the pvp"*, *"shit looks promising though"* — and left. | platform (appearance stage fails and never completes) + NCM (the watchdog's trigger is inside the thing that is broken) | tell them to reconnect, if anyone notices | **FIXED IN CODE — offline-green, NOT deployed, NOT live-validated (L69).** The rule moved out of the event handler and into `ncm/core/world/readiness.lua` (pure, 15 tests, Lua 5.4 + LuaJIT), and `ncm_world/server/main.lua` now only observes and speaks. **(1)** One server-wide sweep every 5 s over `Open77.players.all()` replaces the thread-per-player watchdog, so the question is asked about every connected player whether or not any readiness event ever fired for them. **(2)** "Can play" is now three facts, all required — NCM readiness seen, a position, AND no fresh platform readiness-wait stage. Trip[ABK] satisfied exactly one of the three. **(3)** NCM listens to `open77:appearance:readinessDiagnostic`, the platform's own diagnostic, as evidence only — it never releases a hold. `appearance_failed` is treated as terminal rather than slow (the shell's `announce()` will not retry), so it is advised after ~10 s with different words instead of the 45 s slow-load grace. **(4)** Adopted players survive a resource restart without nagging a healthy server; a fresh stage report still overrides that presumption. **(5)** Leaving while being warned about now leaves a log line with the diagnosis attached — Trip[ABK]'s departure left none. Two console commands: `ncm.readiness` (who is connected, who can actually play, and why not) and `ncm.readiness.test <playerId> [stage]` (inject a stage report so the advise path can be seen firing on a real player — it goes stale by itself and logs recovery). **DEPLOYED — deploy 19, 2026-09-23 01:35 UTC.** §AD1 **PASS**: `readiness: sweep every 5s over the connected list (KB-60); adopted 0 player(s); platform stage listener=on`, `world.readiness` in the started-module list, zero script errors, and **one readiness line across a clean idle boot** — the first evidence that it does not spam. **Live PASS still owed on §AD2–§AD6** (all need a client; `ncm.readiness.test` fires them on demand). §AD7, a genuine `appearance_failed` visitor, is opportunistic: `platform stage listener=on` only proves the registration did not throw, **not** that a second resource actually receives that net event.
| KB-61 | 2026-09-22 | **SEVERE: a wedged NCM panel soft-locks the player out of their own game.** The panel takes mouse AND keyboard focus (`page:setFocus(true, true)` in `setOpen`). Its two escape routes are **F6**, which the focused WebUI page swallows before the keybind sees it, and `panel:closed`, which the page's own JavaScript emits on ESC — so **both escapes depend on the page being healthy, and the page is exactly what has failed.** Owner, 2026-09-22: *"guide isnt even opening now, neither is pause or anything, couldnt capture my mouse. controller kept working but i closed and got a cyberpunk has flatlined error."* | NCM (mine — the escape hatch runs through the thing it is escaping) | controller still works; otherwise kill the process | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED. Fix before the doors open (L91) — this is worse than the Guide bug it came from:** a blank panel is a disappointment, a soft-lock costs someone their session. **The engine never froze** — combat oracle ticked every 5 s and guide payloads kept arriving through 20:38–20:40, and OPEN//77 then unloaded cleanly (`Open77 Phase 0 client unloaded`), so the "flatlined" dialog came from forced teardown, **not** a platform fault. Two fixes needed: **(1)** take input focus only once the page confirms it has drawn the requested panel, never on the request; **(2)** a focus watchdog that releases unconditionally if the page has not signalled within a few seconds. A console command is not a sufficient escape — opening the console needs the keyboard the page has captured. |
| KB-62 | 2026-09-22 | **Three of NCM's four keybinds collide with something else, and the collisions keep moving.** Audit of every `RegisterKeyMapping` across the platform and NCM: **F6** NCM Race Guide vs **POV change** (owner, 2026-09-22 — new, and not present when F6 was chosen); **F9** NCM Messages vs `polyzone.editor` *"PolyZone: toggle editor focus"*; **F10** NCM Vehicle Collection vs `polyzone.add` *"PolyZone: add polygon vertex"*. Also occupied: **F2** `wardrobe.open`, **F7** camera toggle (already cost us one move — Messages went F7→F9 in owner QA, straight into the PolyZone binding). Only **F8** (NCM race graph) is currently uncontested. | platform (bindings added over time) + NCM (four separate bindings for one page) | rebind in settings | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** A static audit has a short shelf life — OPEN//77 is pre-beta and F6 was free when we took it — so the answer is not "find four better keys". **NCM already has ONE WebUI page with four modes** (questionnaire, inbox, guide, collection), so the fix is **one binding plus in-page tabs**, which removes three collisions outright and leaves a single key to defend. Chat twins (`/ncm.guidepage`, `/ncm.msgs`, `/ncm.collection`) already work and are unaffected by any of this. **AMENDED 2026-09-27 (`L180`): a FIFTH binding now exists -- F5, `ncm.build`, the Street Call race builder.** It is not a mode of `panel.html` and therefore could not follow this row's own prescribed fix: the owner's constraint was explicitly that calling a race must not require opening the F6 shell. F5 was chosen against the occupied list above (F2 wardrobe, F6 POV, F7 camera, F9/F10 PolyZone, F8 ours) and that reasoning has exactly the shelf life this row warns about. `/ncm.build` and `/ncm.call` are the durable routes. Re-audit is `IDEA-646`. |
| KB-63 | 2026-09-22 | **`page:send` (client Lua → WebUI page) silently drops large messages — this was the second defect hiding under KB-59.** No error, no return value, no log line: the page simply never receives it. **Proven by the same panel succeeding and failing on size alone.** At 20:56 `lastGuide` was nil, so `panel:show` carried a placeholder of a few dozen bytes and the page drew (`panel drawn: guide`). After a reconnect populated `lastGuide`, the identical call carried ~16.8 KB, never drew, and the watchdog closed it — twice, `PANEL DID NOT DRAW in 2500 ms`. Same code, same page, same session. Note the server→client **net event** carries 16.8 KB without complaint, so this is the WebUI bridge specifically, not the network. | platform (silent drop, no diagnostic) + NCM (sent a whole payload through it) | none | **FIXED — deploy 18, owner-confirmed.** The FIRST attempt (deploy 17) JSON-encoded the payload client-side to split the string, and died on a **second, independent platform limit**: `Open77 script execution budget exceeded` — encoding 16.8 KB in Lua is more work than one client script slice is allowed. It was caught only because `sendBig` logged its own encode failure. The working fix encodes **nothing**: the payload's list (rows / slots / items) is sent in batches of 4 whole table values and the bridge serialises each batch itself. `panel:show` carries only the panel NAME; data follows via `sendBig()`, which JSON-encodes, and above 3,500 bytes splits into `panel:chunk` messages the page reassembles and parses. **Deliberately generic rather than “trim the Guide payload”** — trimming works until the next field is added, and the Vehicle Collection's 27-slot roster will be larger than the Guide. Reassembly failure reports through the KB-61 error path instead of vanishing. **Worth reporting upstream:** a transport that discards messages over a threshold without surfacing anything is very hard to diagnose — it cost three deploys here, and the only reason it was found is that an unrelated reconnect changed the message size. |
| KB-64 | 2026-09-23 | **The KB-60 watchdog warned a healthy player on a normal join — found by its own §AD2 QA, 12 minutes after it shipped.** The owner joined at 01:37:32, the platform's readiness heartbeat fired **once** at 17 s (`appearance_applying`, `reset=complete bootstrap=ready`), NCM went ready at 19 s, and the client then went quiet — which is exactly how that heartbeat says *I stopped waiting*. NCM warned them at **47 s** and told them to reconnect, then logged `reached a playable world after 63s`. **Both numbers were my own timer, not the player's state:** `staleStageS = 45` held a ~15 s heartbeat alive for 45 s. | NCM (mine) | none — the advice is wrong but harmless if ignored | **FIXED — deploy 20, 2026-09-23 01:56 UTC. Awaiting one §AD2 re-run: the next ordinary join must produce no `readiness:` line at all.** Two changes, both in `core/world/readiness.lua`. **(1) `staleStageS` 45 → 25.** The client's diagnostic is a ~15 s heartbeat (`readinessWaitTicks % 15`, counter resets the moment it stops waiting), so a report must not outlive its own interval by three times. **(2) New `progressingGraceS = 90`** — three graces instead of one, because "cannot play" has three prognoses: a **terminal** stage will never resolve (10 s, unchanged — the Trip[ABK] path keeps its fast route), **silence with no readiness** is KB-39's original case (45 s), and a **fresh non-terminal stage means the shell is alive and progressing** (90 s). Four new tests including the owner's join replayed from the log; 163/163 green. **Why this matters more than it looks:** telling a stranger to reconnect while their load is progressing makes them restart a nearly-complete load, so the fix for a bad first impression was itself producing one — on the very first join after it deployed (L91). |
| KB-65 | 2026-09-23 | **The Race Guide panel intermittently fails to answer `panel:show` within 2500 ms, and the watchdog closes it.** Five occurrences tonight: 00:51:29, 00:51:32 (player 1), 01:01:46, 01:02:05 (player 2) — **all four before deploy 21** — and 03:57:58 (player 3, the visitor `A`). **It is NOT a render exception.** A JS throw routes through `guard()` → `panel:error` → `ncm:panel.fault` carrying the exception text and its source location; all five carry the watchdog's own string `did not draw in 2500 ms` with an empty `where`, which is the other branch (`panel.lua:64`). So the page did not crash — it never emitted `panel:drawn` in time. | unknown — WebUI readiness, a dropped `page:send`, or a budget too tight for a cold CEF surface | press the key again; the watchdog releases input, so there is no soft-lock (KB-61 holding) | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED. Cannot be diagnosed from the server log, and guessing is what cost four deploys on KB-59/61/63.** The previous session named the right next step and it was never taken: *"stop guessing at the transport entirely and have the page report what it actually received."* Needed: elapsed-ms between `panel:show` and `panel:drawn`, whether any `panel:list.begin`/`.part` arrived before the deadline, and whether the page was ready at all — reported on BOTH success and failure, because a fault line with no successful baseline says nothing about how often this happens. **Frequency is unknown and currently unknowable:** only failures are logged, so we cannot say whether this is 1-in-5 opens or 1-in-50. **INSTRUMENTATION BUILT 2026-09-23, offline-green, NOT DEPLOYED.** Every panel open now reports itself to the **server** — success and failure in the same shape — because a visitor's client log is on their machine and unreachable, which is why `A`'s fault taught us nothing. Reported: `ms` (request→confirmation, client side), `pageMs` (show→drawn, page side), `sent`/`want` (chunks the bridge put out), `got` (chunks the page actually received), `held` (rows the page holds at draw), `head`. **`sent` vs `got` is a standing KB-63 detector** — the bridge drops large messages silently, and a gap between them is that drop, finally visible. Server keeps a per-panel tally (opens / faults / slowest) and `ncm.panelstats` prints it, so the frequency question becomes answerable. Client input is clamped and rate-limited to one line per player per second. Counts and timestamps only — no encoding, because serialising this transport to measure it is what hit the script-execution budget last time. |
| KB-66 | 2026-09-23 | **`/ncm car <Class>` is the only in-game, player-visible way to get a car, and it grants NO ownership — so every visitor who uses it is still locked out of every race.** `garage.lua` issues a loaner into `issued_vehicle` and **never touches `NcmOwnership`** (grep: zero references). Eligibility reads `NcmOwnership.records()`, so L83 refuses the entry. The player is told to get a car, gets one, drives it, and is still refused. | NCM (mine — two systems that were never joined) | host runs `ncm.grant <pid> <class>` from the console, which a visitor cannot ask for and the host will not see the need for | **NOT FIXED, AND THE ATTEMPTED FIX WAS REMOVED (L102).** The grant-on-issue shipped in deploy 23 and was backed out the same session: on a persistent server a player-typed command that creates an ownable asset is an unmetered faucet, and the economy is the next thing being built. It never wrote a row (`vehicle_asset` = 0, the path never fired), so there was nothing to clean up. §Y1–§Y7 are cancelled. **The real defect is wider than this row claimed:** not "`/ncm car` grants no ownership" but **"there is no discoverable way to get a raceable car"**, which only the F6 Market closes. Treat the onboarding hole as OPEN. Original analysis follows, still accurate on mechanics. This was the single biggest onboarding hole and it is **independent of the economy chain** — it does not need the ledger, supply, or a Market. 13_VEHICLE_COLLECTION.md already licenses the interim: *"`/ncm car` remains an alpha provisioning convenience, not mature acquisition."* Smallest honest fix: `/ncm car` also mints an asset with `acquired_via = 'seed'`, which keeps it forever distinguishable from an earned or purchased car and therefore safely wipeable when real supply lands (L75: alpha data is disposable). Cap it so it cannot be farmed. **Do not** make issued loaners race-eligible instead — that breaks L83's refuse-don't-substitute rule at the point it matters most. |
| KB-67 | 2026-09-23 | **The venue history/practice panel can be left open with nothing able to close it.** Owner, 2026-09-23: hold-E over `history` at race start renders correctly, *"but e doesnt close it, nothing does."* **Root-caused, `client/calendar.lua`:** opening a panel sends `venue:hide` for the option list and sets `lastDrawn = "hidden"` (line 208/212). The teardown branch that would close the panel is guarded by `if lastDrawn ~= "hidden"` (line 259) — which is now false — so when the player starts a race or walks out of radius, `venue:closePanel` is **never sent**, while `panelOpen` is cleared to nil in the same branch. The page keeps rendering the panel, and the tap/hold closer needs `panelOpen` to be truthy, so it no longer fires either. **One variable is doing two jobs:** `lastDrawn` tracks the option LIST, and opening a PANEL sets it to the value that suppresses its own cleanup. | NCM (mine) | rejoin | **FIXED — deploy 23, 2026-09-23 04:39 UTC; awaiting §Y8–§Y10 (MASTER_TEST 2.13).** The teardown now closes on `panelOpen` independently of `lastDrawn`, and clears `panelOpen` only after the close has gone out. Not a soft-lock — the venue page does not take input focus (unlike KB-61) — but it leaves a dead overlay during a race, which is worse than cosmetic because `race.lua` owns that screen. |
| KB-68 | 2026-09-23 | **A purchased vehicle is never delivered: the money leaves, the car is nowhere.** Owner, via the first in-game report ever submitted (`7a579673`, 14:03 UTC): *"bought a mai mai, it didnt appear anywhere but took my money"*. **The purchase itself is CORRECT and atomic** — verified against the authoritative tables, not inferred: ledger `starter_grant +10000` then `vehicle_purchase -2500`, both carrying `idem_key`; asset `10000000-…0001` transferred to the driver with `acquired_via='purchase'`, `granted_by='base_market'`; balance 10000→7500; stock 3→2. Nothing was lost and nothing double-charged. **What is missing is the delivery step**, which the Market's own design named as a prerequisite and which is simply not built yet: *"Ownership cannot remain a database row the player cannot drive."* | NCM — Market workstream (Codex), delivery unbuilt | none in free-roam; the car IS owned, so race entry should still spawn it at the grid | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — belongs to the Market workstream, not this session.** Two separate things to fix and they are not the same size. **(1) Delivery:** a bought asset needs a route into the world — kerbside spawn on purchase is the lower-risk start, because a separate collection step adds a second failure point between paying and having a car, and *"I paid and got nothing"* is the worst bug an economy can have. **(2) The far cheaper half — say something.** The purchase returned no statement of what had happened or where the car was, so a correct transaction was indistinguishable from a theft. Even before delivery exists, confirming *what* was bought and that it is in the player's garage would have prevented this report. **Severity is about trust, not data:** the ledger is right, the ownership is right, and the player still reasonably believes they were robbed. |

---

## Data repair — KB-38 duplicate accomplishments

**Decided (L75): alpha data is disposable.** The code fix stops new duplicates; the wrong rows already written are
deleted outright rather than repaired. No dedupe-by-earliest, no field-size forensics for the solo `first_win`
rows (L67) — they go with the rest.

```sql
DELETE FROM accomplishment WHERE kind IN ('first_finish', 'first_win', 'first_time_trial');
```

Every driver re-earns these on their next race, correctly and once. Nothing else references `accomplishment` rows
by id. Run it on staging whenever convenient; it is not a deploy blocker.

needed or performed. **Earlier kerbside-on-purchase recommendation above is superseded by owner instruction and

player to Machine Index; CALL is asset-keyed and has no persistent writes. Do not close KB-68 before §AB3–AB7.

| KB-87 | 2026-09-23 | **Suspected ~20% odometer overcount on the first live mileage run.** MaiMai #0000001 went 3.6 km -> 11.5 km over Tip->Trench ("Ukulele"), nominally a 6.6 km route: +7.9 km against +6.6 km. | investigated; **the headline figure is not an overcount** | n/a | **THE 6.6 KM IS A STRAIGHT LINE, NOT A ROAD DISTANCE.** `course.routes.resolve` sets `distanceM = planarDistance(start, finish)` and only sums legs when a route authors `intermediate` points. `root_tip_1035_3652__to__trench_2899_2711` authors none (the race ran with `gates_total = 2`), so its distance is crow-flies between two nodes: recomputed exactly, **6,630 m**. It is also PLANAR, ignoring the climb from z=14 to z=20 and the tunnel between. 7,900 m of road for a 6,630 m straight line is a circuity factor of **1.19**, which is normal-to-low for driving between two points in a city. The authored description ("A 6.6 km cross-city run") was written from that same crow-flies number, which is what made it read as ground truth. **One writer only** (`odometer.lua`), additive SQL, no second loop, no client contribution, no unit error; 1 Hz straight-line sampling under-measures curves if anything. **Existing mileage was NOT altered: 11,903 m before and after deploy.** |
| KB-88 | 2026-09-23 | **Stationary physics jitter did accumulate real distance.** The odometer's only stationary guard was a 0.25 m positional floor per 1 Hz sample -- which is 0.9 km/h, uncomfortably close to a car genuinely creeping, so a parked car squirming by ~0.5 m/s cleared it and banked roughly 1.9 km for every hour parked. The owner observed exactly this. | NCM odometer | none | **FIXED AND DEPLOYED (prospective only); LIVE RE-CHECK OWED.** Speed is now an independent witness: a vehicle reporting under 0.2 m/s (0.72 km/h) has its positional delta rejected as `stationary_by_speed`, and a delta beyond what the reported speed could cover is CAPPED rather than discarded, so a glitch inside a real journey loses its excess and not the whole second. Legitimate creeping (3-8 km/h) is far above the threshold and counts in full -- asserted by test. A build with no speed reading falls back to the old positional floor, and the server logs the vehicle snapshot's field list once per boot so the available signals are never a guess again. Bounded diagnostics: `ncm.odo.trace <assetId> [minutes]` logs raw/accepted/rejected/dt/speed per sample for one asset and expires on its own. |

| KB-92 | 2026-09-24 | **One `database_schema_error` (SQL 1146) fires on every boot where the schema is behind the code.** Seen on the Windows development box's first `.87` boot, whose database sat at migration 005 while the code carried 023. The error lands at `T+0.037s`, between `database.isReady at start -> true` and `database reachable`, i.e. **before** `NcmMigrations.apply()` has run (`T+0.423s`). `main.lua` itself is correctly ordered -- migrations run inside the `MySQL.ready` callback before any seeding -- so the query comes from some other start-time path that does not wait for that callback. Boot then completed perfectly: 18 migrations applied, every subsystem started, exactly one `[ERR]` in the whole log. | NCM start-time ordering (site not yet identified) | none needed; it is non-fatal | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** Invisible in production, which is always already migrated -- it only appears when the schema is behind, which is the state of **every fresh install and every rebuilt environment**. | KB-93 | 2026-09-24 | **`ncm_league/open77.lua` listed `server/schema/022_supply_dynamics.lua` twice**, the second time *after* 023, so the manifest read `...021, 022, 023, 022`. | NCM manifest | n/a | **FIXED 2026-09-24.** Harmless only by accident: every schema file appends unconditionally with `NcmSchema[#NcmSchema + 1]`, so a genuine double load would have produced 24 entries. Had it not -- or if that behaviour ever changes -- `NcmMigrations.apply()` would insert version 22 into `schema_migrations` **twice in one run**, hit the primary key, and return `migration_failed`, halting NCM at boot on any database not already past 022. Removed the stray line. |
| KB-94 | 2026-09-24 | **[NCM DEVELOPMENT]** **Market and Machine Index load empty on the dev box; Race Guide and Help are fine.** Owner, live on the dev server: *"market does not load in my dev server and neither does machine index. race guide and help load fine."* Server evidence: `[ncm_league/market] payload failed player=1: nil` repeating, and `panel=collection ... chunks sent=0/0 got=0 head=0` — the KB-86 signature of a panel that opened with no payload to batch. **Root cause: `ncm_world` declares its OWN `serverId` tunable** (`identity.lua:19`, default `ncm-dev`) **separate from `ncm_league`'s** (`calendar.lua:26`). When the dev box was stood up (L121) only the `ncm_league` block was set in `tunables.json`, so **identity minted under `ncm-dev` while assets, markets and production live under `ncm-devbox`** — measured: `ncm_driver_binding` 1 row under `ncm-dev`, `vehicle_asset` 18 rows under `ncm-devbox`. `NcmMarket.payload` opens with `local d = driver(pid); if not d then return nil, "no_driver" end`, and the league's binding lookup filters `server_id = 'ncm-devbox'`, so it finds nothing. **Guide and Help are unaffected because neither is driver-scoped** — the Guide is a server-wide calendar and Help is static, which is exactly the split the owner observed. | **NCM Development only — but the same split is possible on Online** (`IDEA-305`); mine, introduced standing up the dev box at L121 | none in-game | **FIXED AND RESTARTED 2026-09-24 07:58 (owner off the server).** `tools/open77/server/tunables.json` now sets `ncm_world.serverId = ncm-devbox`; the value is read at boot, so it takes effect on the next restart (or set it live in Warden, since the tunable is `apply = "live"`). Restarted once the owner was off: clean boot, migration 024 applied, **zero `[ERR]` lines** -- which incidentally confirms KB-92's diagnosis, since that `SQL 1146` fires only when the schema is behind and did not appear on an already-migrated boot. After the restart a fresh identity mints under `ncm-devbox`; the one stale `ncm-dev` binding/profile pair created today is an orphan and can be relabelled the way the 2026-09-24 legacy rows were, or left. **No production change and no Online risk from this fix.** |
| KB-95 | 2026-09-24 | **A diagnosable failure was logged as `nil`, which is what made KB-94 take a log dive instead of a glance.** `NcmMarket.push` does `local ok, payload = pcall(NcmMarket.payload, pid)` and on failure logs `tostring(payload)`. But `payload` is the **first** return value, which is `nil` on every refusal — the actual reason (`"no_driver"`) is the **second**, and is discarded. So a precise, already-computed explanation printed as the least informative string available. | NCM logging (`ncm_league/market`) | read the code to find the refusal reasons | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** One-line fix: capture and log the second return (`local ok, payload, why = pcall(...)`). A refusal that knows why it refused should never print `nil`. |
| KB-96 | 2026-09-24 | **[NCM DEVELOPMENT]** **The odometer panel shows a bare unlabelled serial, so it reads as disagreeing with other surfaces.** Owner: *"odometer shows the hella serial as #18 although machine index says correctly 03."* **The odometer is not wrong.** Measured: the owned Hella `10000000-...` carries **three** ordinals, exactly as `IDEA-226` designed — **NCM global #18, Archer #4, `archer_hella` #4** — and the odometer renders `production_ordinal`, the global one, which is truthful. But it renders it as a naked `#18` with nothing saying *which history it counts*, and `IDEA-226` deliberately created three parallel histories. A number with no sequence name is guaranteed to look like it contradicts the next surface that shows a different one. **Note `03` matches none of the three** (global 18, maker 4, model 4), and the Machine Index as authored renders no serial at all — it is model-level: category, status, source, first encounter, encounter count, plus an `N / total DISCOVERED` counter. So the `03` is probably a count or list position being read as a serial, **which is the same ambiguity from the other direction.** | NCM presentation (`odometer.lua` publishes `ord = production_ordinal`; the panel renders it bare) | none | **ANSWERED BY SCREENSHOT 2026-09-24 — nothing is stale, and the real defect is worse than a mismatch.** The Machine Index detail pane renders **`ASSET 10000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000003`** — so `003` is the **tail of the asset UUID**, not a serial at all. (My own query hid this: I selected `LEFT(asset_id,8)`, which truncates every id to the identical prefix `10000000`.) So the two panels show **two different identifiers, neither labelled as what it is**: the odometer shows the NCM global ordinal `#18`, the Machine Index shows a raw UUID. **Ordinals verified sound** -- 19 assets, 19 distinct globals, contiguous 1-19; Hella model ordinals 1-5, MaiMai 1-8, Galena 1-6, no gaps or duplicates. **The presentation defect: a raw UUID has no business in a player-facing panel.** It is a database key, it is not a name, and showing it invites exactly this reading. The fix is to name the sequence rather than change the number: `NCM #0000018` and `HELLA #0004` are both true and neither can be mistaken for the other. `data.vehicle_identity.formatOrdinal(n, pad)` already exists for exactly this and **is currently called only by tests** — the formatter was written and never wired. |
| KB-97 | 2026-09-24 | **Personalised NCM player state-bag mirrors are replicated to every connected player, not to the subject.** OPEN//77 documents the audience precisely: a `player(id)` bag reaches *"every player in that player's routing bucket, including the subject"*, and the platform frames bags as carrying *"gameplay secrets"* with the bucket as the confidentiality boundary. **NCM never sets a routing bucket** -- it reads them in `collection.lua`, `help.lua` and the weather probe, and writes one nowhere (`weather.lua`: *"Event bucket policy is deliberately a later consumer"*). So all players share bucket 0 and every personalised mirror is broadcast. Affected: **`ncmInbox`** (message and seat-offer bodies), **`ncmOdo`** (the serialised `assetId`, serial and lifetime distance of the machine a player occupies), **`ncmGuide.iOwn`/`carRefusal`** (garage composition and eligibility), **`ncmHistory.mine`/`myBestMs`** (personal results), **`ncmVenue.options`**. `ncmEntry`, `ncmResult`, `ncmPractice`, `ncmOnboarding` and `ncmHelp` are correctly public or harmless. | NCM replication channel choice (not a platform defect -- OPEN//77 documents this and offers the alternative) | none needed today | **FIXED 2026-09-24 (four of five); `ncmVenue.options` deliberately deferred.** Owner: *"yup do them"*. `ncmGuide` turned out to be **pure duplication** -- the server already sent the identical payload on the targeted `ncm:guide.payload` event and the client already handled it, so the broadcast copy was deleted outright. `ncmInbox` -> `ncm:inbox.payload`. `ncmOdo` -> `ncm:odo`. `ncmHistory` -> `ncm:venue.history`, and `ncmPractice` moved with it (`ncm:venue.practice`) so the client keeps one rule rather than two for the same interaction. **A bug class was removed rather than guarded:** history and practice are pushed only when the driver picks that option, so an event IS the request -- which deletes the `at`-stamp adoption rule that existed solely because a persisted bag survives into the next session and reads as a fresh request. That is the root of KB-67 and the *"there is no race panel to enter the race"* QA. Gate green, 265 core tests per interpreter, exit 0; clean boot, zero `[ERR]`. **Original audit follows.** Full field-by-field classification in `29_STATE_BAG_AUDIENCE_AUDIT.md`. **No evidence of exploitation and not a live incident**: the stock client reads only its own keys and Online has one player. **The fix is small and needs no new networking** -- NCM already has a targeted per-player channel (`TriggerClientEvent("ncm:*.payload", pid, ...)`) and already uses it for its richest surfaces (Market, Machine Index, Guide, Help). The correction is moving about five fields from a channel NCM has to another channel NCM has. Each needs a matching client read change, so none was applied. |

New `tools/test-market-knowledge.lua` executes the real adapter under both supported Lua runtimes and asserts the exact seven-value contract. Full gate green; commit `0406562` pushed before promotion; fresh rollback artifacts `ncm-db-20260925-013053.sql.gz` and `ncm-resources-20260925-013053.tar.gz`; deployed SHA-256 matches local; service active, HTTP 200, schema 30, no post-restart errors and all canonical row counts preserved. | KB-101 | 2026-09-25 | **[NCM ONLINE] NCM saves a player's position before they are incarnated, then restores them to it -- the world origin.** `startLocationSweep` runs over `Open77.players.all`, which includes players who have connected but have no puppet yet, and `saveLocation` has no incarnation guard: `Location.valid` checks only finiteness and `abs(coord) < 100000`, and `0.0, 3.6, 0.5` passes both. | KB-103 | 2026-09-25 | **The wheel went permanently silent after the first `stop()`, and re-arming was the only cure.** `SDL_HapticRunEffect` was called exactly once per effect, at arm time; `write()` only ever called `SDL_HapticUpdateEffect`; `stop()` called `SDL_HapticStopEffect` on everything. **Updating a stopped effect changes its parameters and produces no force**, and nothing ever ran them again. `pulse()` ends in `stop()`, so Test Pulse worked exactly once. `write()`'s own exception handler calls `stop()`, so any transient silenced the wheel for the rest of the session. `set_condition` had the identical trap on a path `write()` does not cover -- moving the CENTRING slider with the car stationary. | NCM companion (`ncm-wheel.py`, `Output`) | unarm and re-arm | **FIXED AND REGRESSION-COVERED 2026-09-25.** A `_playing` flag separate from effect existence; `_resume()` re-runs stopped effects on the next `write()` or `set_condition`, idempotently, and clears the change-cache so the first frame back is not suppressed as a no-op. **Two of monki's six symptoms were this one bug** -- *"Test Pulse works only once"* and *"centering... after a couple seconds my wheel isn't getting FFB anymore"* -- and his workaround was the diagnosis. `test_resume.py` drives a fake haptic device, counts SDL calls and **fails against the pre-fix source** with `2 update(s) went to stopped effects`; it now runs in CI before the build. The bug produced no error, no log line, and reads as correct in the source. |
| KB-104 | 2026-09-25 | **Force resolution was quantised against the wrong scale, so the whole usable range arrived in tens of steps.** `write()` skipped any change below `0.005` **absolute** -- but the value it guards has already been clamped to the device ceiling. The threshold existed only to avoid redundant USB writes, which at 30 Hz was never a real cost. monki: *"very grainy, like having sand in the gears"* -- **a heavy gear-drive wheel's inertia smooths 40 steps into continuity and a lighter belt wheel shows you every stair**, which is why a year of testing on a G29 never surfaced it. Direct drive at **12 steps** would have been far worse and is the class we ship most cautiously. Asserted by `test_resume.py`. |
