Dev now boots 38 resources, schema 30 / `pending=[]`, zero `[ERR]`, empty stderr, health 200, zero freeroam references, market ready and race control started. **The more serious half is the guard.** `tools/staging/check-load-list.py` read the load list with `"([A-Za-z0-9_]+)"`, which cannot match `ncm/*` -- the slash and star fall outside the class -- so the one tool whose stated purpose is catching *"a healthy-looking server with no NCM"* was **structurally incapable of seeing NCM's own entry**. It never failed; it was blind, and it reported `load list OK` throughout. Now: any quoted entry matches, globs are expanded against the resources on disk, an empty glob is itself an error, and the KB-51 question is asked **directly** rather than inferred -- no `ncm_*` resource in the expanded set is a hard failure. Run against the broken dev config it reported `NCM IS NOT IN THE LOAD LIST` on the first try. **A diagnostic that cannot express the failure it was written for is worse than none**, because its silence reads as evidence. |

| KB-113 | 2026-09-25 | **L110 stored the join-window placeholder puppet as a location and then teleported the player into it: the owner fell through the world, and every join spawned in twice.** During the join window the client reports a placeholder position at the world origin. `saveLocation` had no readiness gate, so the 20 s sweep stored `(0, 3.6, 0)`; `restoreLocation` read it back 2.6 s later, passed every guard -- `Location.valid` is finiteness and a +/-100000 bound, so the origin is a perfectly legal place -- and teleported the player there. **`fresh` could not have caught it:** the readiness sweep logged `in-world=yes` (which IS `p.fresh == true`) for the same player it simultaneously called `CANNOT PLAY`. | KB-124 | 2026-09-25 | **The server prunes mod-store entries it did not import itself, silently, on restart -- taking the declaration with them.** `publish-carrier.py` wrote `<sha>.blob` and `<sha>.json` into `.open77/mods/` by hand and declared the package. It served correctly: the manifest reported `ncm-carrier 0.1.0` at digest `78c37893...`. After the next restart **both store files were gone and `requiredMods.packages` was empty**, digest back to the empty-set `e3b0c442...`. The Warden-imported RB15 and livery blobs sitting beside them were untouched. | NCM tooling | import packages through Warden, then declare | **FIXED 2026-09-25 by making the tool stop guessing. CAUSE NOT ESTABLISHED.** The first write-up of this row claimed the store is Warden's private catalogue and that only Warden may fill it. **That is wrong and the owner corrected it:** Production's ArchiveXL and TweakXL were placed and configured without Warden, over HTTPS, and worked -- `configure-required-mods-https.py` is built around exactly that. So there is more than one legitimate way in, and *why* the hand-written entry vanished remains unproven. **A candidate, found afterwards and never ruled out:** Warden derives its own id and version from the file name -- `ncm-carrier-0.1.0.zip` became id `ncm-carrier-0-1-0` at version `1.0.0`, not `ncm-carrier` at `0.1.0`. A declaration naming an id the store does not hold cannot resolve, and a self-consistent pair of hand-written files can still disagree with whatever else the server reconciles against. The fix is therefore not a theory about ownership: `publish-carrier.py` reads the store and declares **exactly the id and version it finds there**, and refuses when the blob is absent rather than inventing it. `--import-blob` keeps the old behaviour for an operator who knows why they want it. **Lesson: when one observation admits several explanations, fix the guessing, not the guess.** |
| KB-125 | 2026-09-25 | **A direct-IP join to Development delivered no required mods at all, because the server advertised its blob URL as private-LAN plain HTTP.** Two joins at 15:07 produced `OP77_WORLD_MODS_DIGEST=-` (empty) and `modstack: projected 348 file(s) - 0 added, 0 repaired, 44 removed`, with **no consent prompt and no error**. The real blocker is `KB-126`: the world is `Visibility=Unlisted` and the owner connects by raw address, and a direct-IP join never asks for the mod manifest. The URL finding stands on its own merits -- see `KB-112`, it would have failed at the next step -- but **it was diagnosed as the cause on one correlated observation and it was not the cause.** Original text follows. This is `KB-112` biting the box it explicitly warned about -- *"Development still uses private-LAN HTTP"* -- and it was never acted on. The server picks its own interface, so `publicBaseUrl` defaulted to `http://10.0.0.70:11779/`; launchers `.89`/`.100` null every non-loopback `http:` mod URL, before download, cache, hash or projection. **The whole failure is invisible from the server side**: the manifest is correct, the blob is fetchable, and the player simply never sees a package. New tool `tools/staging/set-download-url.py` sets it and *refuses* a non-loopback HTTP value rather than letting it be configured again. **The 44 removed files were ArchiveXL's and TweakXL's DLLs**, which the world no longer requires -- the removal was correct, the absence of an addition was the bug. |

| KB-136 | 2026-09-25 | **The first successful 0.1.1 session received no record projection in the carrier despite the server publishing 11 projection records.** Server boot published 11; client `ncm_world` ran and the bridge connected; after several minutes `ncm_carrier.log` contained no `projection v1` line. The cause was not any of the three things I predicted.** The original instruction -- instrument the transport rather than ask the owner to judge feel -- was right and is now done. **First, a correction to how this row has been summarised elsewhere.** A later session described the evidence as *"total silence -- no log line from either branch of the loop"*, which reads as though the payload was observed to be absent. **It was not, and this row already said so:** both client loops are written `if records then ... end` with no else, so silence proves only that `records` was falsy. It cannot tell apart (a) the payload never replicated, (b) it replicated but the nested table was dropped, (c) it replicated and the map arrived **empty**, or (d) it arrived fine and the bug is downstream. Case (c) is easy to miss and would look identical: an empty table is truthy, the `for` loop runs zero times, nothing is logged. **Four causes, four different fixes.** **What shipped.** `server/state_probe.lua` publishes five payloads that vary one property at a time -- a bare scalar, a table holding an array (`ncmCalendar`'s known-good shape), a string-keyed map, the same map keyed `Vehicle.v_...` (**the prime suspect: both broken payloads are keyed by TweakDB record path, which contains a `.`**), and an array of eleven realistic envelopes to separate size and depth from shape. `client/state_probe.lua` reports what each one looks like on arrival, including when the answer is `nil`, and logs only on change. **And a candidate fix, shipped alongside rather than after.** Both `ncmCeilings` and `ncmProjections` now publish an `entries` array **in addition to** the historical map, and both client readers prefer `entries` and fall back to `records`, normalising back to a record-keyed table so no downstream logic changed. Both client loops now report what they saw. If the shape is the cause, one drive both diagnoses and repairs it; if it is not, the probe says which property is. **One fact that narrows it already:** `fleet()` in `ncm_world/server/machine.lua` reads `GlobalState.ncmCeilings.records` **server-side** and gets its 11 records, so the map replicates server-to-server. The failure is strictly server-to-client. And `ncmProjections` is published and read inside the **same resource**, so it is not a cross-resource problem either. |
| KB-137 | 2026-09-25 | **The `.87 -> .102` upgrade took the whitelist down and then made it unfixable, and neither half is a platform bug.** Two distinct faults from one run of `upgrade-open77.sh`. **(1) The access list was not preserved.** `access.json` holds the whitelist and ban list, lives at the server tree root, and is written by the server at runtime -- it is not in the package manifest. The preservation list carried `server.jsonc`, `acl.jsonc`, `.open77` and the branding images, but not it, so the swap left the only copy in `/opt/ncm/backups/server-20260925T200746Z/access.json` and the live tree started with no access state at all. **This is KB-52 recurring**, and KB-52 already asked for a post-upgrade whitelist check that was never added. **(2) Re-adding an identity in Warden then failed, because the new tree is root-owned.** The service runs as `ubuntu` (`systemd User=ubuntu`); the upgrade was run under `sudo`, so `tar` unpacked 371 root-owned files and `/opt/ncm/server` itself became `root:root drwxr-xr-x`, while the five preserved items stayed `ubuntu`. The server writes the access list by temp-file-then-rename inside its own working directory, so every save died on `Access to the path '/opt/ncm/server/access.json.tmp' is denied.` Warden surfaced that as *"access list could not be saved; identity was not added"* and, notably, **the API still answered `200`** -- six failed `access.allow` attempts on the owner's own GUID `ae3e440b-...` are audited `ok:false` at seq 119-124 while the HTTP layer reported success. Nothing else looked wrong: the server was active, Warden loaded, `GET /api/access` returned `200` throughout. | NCM staging tooling (both halves; the platform behaved correctly and logged precisely) | `sudo chown -R ubuntu:ubuntu /opt/ncm/server`, restore `access.json` from the pre-upgrade backup, then add the identity **before** enabling the whitelist -- enabling with an empty list refuses everyone, including the owner | **FIXED IN TOOLING; live restore pending an operator write.** `upgrade-open77.sh` now preserves `access.json`, `chown -R`s the candidate to the systemd service user before the swap, and ends with an access guard that fails the upgrade when the tree is not writable by that user and warns loudly when no access list carried over. The general lesson is the same one KB-51, KB-52 and KB-130 each taught once: **an upgrade that leaves the process running has not been verified**, because every one of these faults was invisible until someone tried to use the thing it broke. |

| KB-143 | 2026-09-26 | **Three separate statistical faults in the probe's own reporting, each of which produced a confident and wrong reading rather than a null one.** **(1) Truncation bias.** Force accumulation used `static_cast<int64_t>(force * 100.0f)`, which truncates toward zero and therefore biases a mixed-sign sum *downward by the same order as the signal*, manufacturing a plausible 0.10-0.20 ratio out of noise. **(2) Misaligned velocity sampling.** The tracked body's velocity was differenced from `g_bestVel` -- the velocity at the moment of the *fastest* sample in a window, not at report time -- so two unrelated instants were differenced across a window boundary. **(3) Uncontrolled window duration.** §AV2's first pass compared *maxima* across windows of 95 s and 52 s and read the difference as a 138 -> 121 collapse in lateral capability, symmetric across plus and minus bias, which is exactly the signature of load-dependent grip. **It was an artefact of sample count.** Duration-controlled percentiles put the three settings within 5 percent of each other, and the finding inverted from a strong positive to a clean negative. | NCM carrier (`vehicle/physics_probe.cpp`) | **ALL THREE FIXED before §AV2 was reported.** `llround` at 1e6 replaces the truncating cast; velocity is captured on the tracked body at report time; and capability is reported as duration-controlled median, p75 and p90 rather than as a maximum. | **Closed. The methodology rule it produced is the durable part:** never compare extrema across windows of different length, and prefer an accumulated regression over a sampled value -- the same correction that rescued the yaw measurement after three failed attempts at 1 Hz sampling. Fault (3) is the serious one: (1) and (2) produced numbers that looked odd, while (3) produced a number that looked like the discovery we were hoping for, and only a duration control caught it. |

| KB-147 | 2026-09-26 | **FIXED 2026-09-26 in Carrier 0.1.22 + `ncm_world`.** The bridge now walks a four-port range (`38480-38483`) and logs which one it took and why; the client scans the same range instead of one fixed URL. **The half that matters is the refusal, not the walk.** Once the port is not fixed, a scanning client will knock on the OTHER game's carrier whenever that one bound first -- and being served would put one player's Parts into the other player's physics, which is `KB-146` reintroduced by the fix for this row. A PID it cannot determine is allowed through, deliberately: the table is a snapshot and a just-accepted connection can be missing from it, so treating "I could not tell" as "wrong process" would break the ordinary single-client case to defend a two-client one. The client also now handles a page that **loads but never handshakes** -- previously `surface` was non-nil so `bringUp` never ran again and it waited forever, which is exactly what a refusal looks like from that side. `/ncm.ffb url <u>` pins and stops the scan; `/ncm.ffb auto` resumes it. The old error message, which blamed the retired Python companion for a generic errno, now names the range tried and the winsock error. **Original observation, kept:** two game instances on one PC cannot both run the carrier bridge: it binds a fixed loopback port and the second bind fails, so the second client has no carrier at all.** `bridge/server.cpp` binds `127.0.0.1:38480` with no `SO_REUSEADDR`, and the port comes from `ncm_carrier.json` in the DLL directory -- which both instances share, because they are the same game install. I initially read the refused pid as the main client; the `cache/web/instances/<pid>/` directories proved the opposite -- the refused one had no instance directory at all (it is the `KB-4` client), and the working one owned the carrier. **The nine-second refusal cadence was the clue**: four ports times two seconds. The fix keeps the backstop but raises it to 20 s and adds the real mechanism -- `ncm:ffb.closed` before any successful handshake, three times, advances in about six seconds. Three and not one, because the page retries on its own every two seconds and the first attempt can legitimately lose a race with a carrier still coming up. **Lesson worth keeping: a timeout expressed in ticks is meaningless without the tick period, and I wrote a comment asserting 'about two seconds' as though that settled it rather than checking what the loop actually waits.** |

The fix walks the peer's parent chain (bounded to 8, because reused pids can make a cycle and an unbounded walk inside an accept loop is a hang) and accepts anything descended from this process, so a second game's WebHost still resolves to a different root and is still refused. **Lesson: a guard that has never let anything through is not proven, it is untested -- and I shipped this one with its own plausible-looking log line as the only evidence it worked.** |

Cause: Economy v1 (`L169`) put a per-row `economy` block on every Guide row -- about 270 bytes x 17 rows -- which took the payload from the last measured-good 17,918 bytes back to roughly 22.5 KB, the same size that broke it in `KB-86`. **The deeper fault is that the server-to-client hop was never bounded.** `sendBig` batches rows for the WebUI bridge only, and cannot rescue a payload that never reached the client; the Market hop was bounded after exactly this failure (`KB-153`) but the Guide's was not. `PAYLOAD_WARN_BYTES` was set correctly at 18 KB this time and would have warned -- it is a tripwire, not a fix, and shrinking the payload is a fix with a shelf life. | NCM Guide transport; regression introduced by `L169` | none -- the Guide is unusable | **FIXED AND DEPLOYED ONLINE 2026-09-27 19:43 UTC; CLIENT-SIDE LIVE PASS OWED (`MASTER_TEST` §BK).** The deployed server confirms both the cause and the fix in one pair of lines: `19:10:13 guide published player=1 rows=16 bytes=21287` on the old build, then `19:48:30 guide published player=1 rows=17 bytes=22574 head=385 packets=5` on the new one. **22,574 bytes is the payload that was disappearing** -- the same ~22.5 KB as `KB-86` -- and the identical 16-17 rows measured 16,826-17,918 bytes before Economy v1, so the per-row `economy` block added ~4.5 KB exactly as predicted. No `HEAD LARGE`, `ROW OVERSIZED` or `NOT ENCODABLE` line, and no script error. What is still unverified is the client half: that the five packets reassemble and draw. Four parts. (a) `ncm:guide.payload` is replaced by `ncm:guide.stream.begin` plus numbered `ncm:guide.stream.part` packets, assembled on the client before anything is shown, with the legacy whole-payload receiver kept for one mixed-version restart window. (b) Packets are packed by **measured bytes** (6 KB budget), not by row count -- `vocabulary.chunks.partitionBytes` -- because a row-count budget cannot see rows that grow, which is what broke `KB-86` and this at the identical 17 rows. A row over budget is isolated and logged rather than sent silently. (c) The **head** is the one unsplittable part, and is exactly where `KB-86`'s twenty rows of history sat, so it has its own tripwire. (d) **The panel no longer invents a date.** With no host time it says `NO HOST TIME YET` and the empty list blames the transport ("No schedule has reached this panel") instead of the calendar; the driver card withholds its countdown rather than reporting 56 years. Asserted in `test_chunks`, in `panel-render-check` (which must run the unseeded case first, since `guideOffset` is module state), and in `ncm-stub`'s Guide scenario against the real stream -- that scenario had been failing on its first assertion since `KB-97` removed the `ncmGuide` bag it read, so the Guide transport had no offline coverage at all (`IDEA-596`). |

Wagers were under-rewarded at high tier relative to intent, never over-rewarded, so no economy was inflated | **The lesson is the same one as `KB-155`: a lookup with a default can never report having missed.** `byTier[tostring(career.tier or "")] or 0.0` is indistinguishable from a tier that legitimately contributes nothing. | KB-162 | 2026-09-27 | **I reported a platform capability as BLOCKED three days after OPEN//77 shipped it, because I treated a stale index's silence as evidence of absence.** The character/PvE reconnaissance concluded *"shared traffic is BLOCKED — client-local, no canonical id, invisible to other clients"*. OPEN//77's changelog of **2026-09-24**, *"AI traffic rolls into Night City"*, leads with **Traffic Sync**: *"Shared AI traffic is now in: cars can join and drive in traffic with synchronized vehicle movement across players."* The owner caught it from the changelog. **Two compounding errors.** (a) I conflated *vanilla REDengine ambient population traffic*, which genuinely is per-client, with *shared AI traffic built from server-created canonical vehicles*, which is a different mechanism that has existed since op77.54/.57 — `Open77.vehicles.ai.joinTraffic`, whose own guide says in as many words that it *"uses indefinite road-following, **not ambient population spawning**"* and that *"one nearby ready client executes native driving; observers receive replicated motion"*. The quote I built BLOCKED on was true and was about the other thing. (b) I queried the devkit MCP, was told *"no index published for 2.31.13+op77.102; serving 2.31.13+op77.78"*, and reported what .78 did not contain as what the platform cannot do. | me, in `docs/research/open77/CHARACTER_ARCHAEOLOGY_AND_PVE_SUBSTRATE.md` | **corrected in place**: section VII.7 is rewritten with the correction retained as a record, the feasibility table now separates vanilla ambient traffic (per-client) from shared AI traffic (AVAILABLE), and the document's proof-ladder preamble now warns that an absent card means the index does not know | **The index is 24 builds behind staging and nothing in the workspace says so at the point of use.** `NOW.md` already carried the habit — *"when the index lags the deployed build, grep the binary"* — and I did grep the binary, which showed `isInTraffic`, `trafficVehicle`, `HasTrafficSlot`, `HoldsTrafficLane`; I read those as ambiguous instead of as a prompt to check the release channel. **The rule this sets: a BLOCKED verdict on platform capability requires a positive source — a card that says no, or a guide that states the limit — never the absence of a card.** UNKNOWN is the honest verdict when the index is stale, and it costs nothing to say. Also worth keeping: the `.87` server package in the workspace root has no traffic resource, and the devblog describes *"the traffic resource"*. **AMENDED 2026-09-27 after inspecting the `op77.102` package the owner supplied.** Two corrections to this row's own advice. (1) The `.102` package **also** has no traffic resource — the resource set is identical to `.87` (`gamemodes`, `open77_equipment`, `open77_wardrobe`, `polyzone`, `system`) — so "read the traffic resource" was not an available action. What `.102` does add is operator documentation that was never shipped before (`server-configuration.md`, `metrics.md`, `rcon.md`), and those confirm the networking half of the release is real and configurable (`replication.trafficLanes`, `timedMotionWindowMs`, `timedVehicleValidationTicks`, `timedStarvationShareMs`) and that the traffic budget is **measurable** (`open77_entities{kind}`, active simulators, maximum per-simulator vehicle count, AI job states). (2) **"Grep the binary" is not a reliable oracle for Lua API presence, and this row previously implied it was.** `joinTraffic`, `attachDriver`, `driveTo`, `whenReady` and `npcs.speak` appear as plain strings in **no** server binary, yet every one of them is officially documented and shipping — binding names are not stored as literals. So a grep finding nothing proves nothing, and concluding "there is no `Open77.traffic.*`" from its absence would have been **the same error as the original BLOCKED verdict wearing a new costume**. A grep hit is evidence; a grep miss is not. **The standing rule, restated:** a capability claim in either direction needs a positive source — a card, a guide sentence, a shipped file, or an executed call. When none is available the honest verdict is UNKNOWN and the cheapest next step is usually to *run it*, not to read more. |

| KB-165 | 2026-09-27 | **The ambient population service would have emptied Night City permanently — the exact opposite of what it was built for — and I shipped it that way until the owner asked the obvious question.** Two compounding defects. (b) `isHeldClear` held a corridor for every **scheduled** slot whether or not anyone had entered, and for fifteen guessed minutes past a start that may never have produced a session. **The arithmetic makes it total, not marginal:** the five shipped street series run 20/30/60/180-minute cadences at staggered offsets — **21 starts in any three-hour window** — and the per-slot hold intervals merge into **one continuous interval, 100% of the clock**. A bucket a race has to itself still is, where the only players it reaches are racing — which is `IDEA-638`'s case, now ready for it. `ambientVanillaSuppressShared` is the explicit escape hatch for a private server and defaults **off**. `isHeldClear` now requires a non-zero **entrant count** when one is given and holds a scheduled row only to `startsAt + graceSeconds` (120 s, enough for the T-60 session to form and take over), after which only the live session board justifies a corridor. **Seven new core assertions and the rewritten `ncm-stub ambient` scenario pin both rules, and the scenario is mutation-checked against a corridor with no entrants.** Proof stays owed at §BL9. **The lesson is not "read your own comments":** I had the constraint written down correctly and never multiplied it by the race cadence. A bucket-wide switch in a world with a race every twenty minutes is a permanent switch, and nothing in the tests would ever have said so — every one of them looked at a single race. |

| KB-167 | 2026-09-28 | **The quest graph's trigger budget silently rationed away the beats the player had just asked for, and it took a host harness to see it.** Three defects in `ncm_league/server/graph.lua`, none visible to the core tests, all found within minutes of `tools/test-quest-graph.lua` existing. (c) **A world-initiated beat repeated forever**: after the player walked Rico's Needle thread to its end, the trigger for its first beat was still satisfied, because the authored condition watches `needleKnown`/`needleDeferred` and asking for an introduction to somebody unauthored sets neither. (b) `Known` now means `lastSpokeAt ~= nil` — the player has actually heard from them — which is both true and what makes a rationed beat retryable. (c) The host records each pushed node and never pushes it again; re-entry is a player action (§0.2), never the clock. **The lesson is about where the tests were, not about the budget.** All three defects live in the adapter between authored data and the database, and 70 offline assertions over the core modules could not reach any of them — the same shape as `KB-159`. `tools/test-*.lua` joins the gate by existing, which is exactly why writing one first is cheap. |
| KB-168 | 2026-09-28 | **A nil SQL parameter is not NULL to OPEN//77 -- it is a HOLE -- and three shipped statements had been failing on the live server because of it, one of them silently losing money.** The bridge counts `?` against `#values`, and a Lua array with a nil near its end has no dependable length: `{a,b,c,nil,nil,nil}` is 3. So the statement is refused with `parameter_count_mismatch` rather than storing NULL. Found in the live log while verifying the quest-graph deploy. Three instances, in increasing severity. (a) **`player_message_exposure`** (`server/communication.lua`) has failed on **every write since the table shipped** -- a fresh row has two nil timestamps. (b) **`ledger`, the payout line** (`server/economy.lua`) raised an **uncaught** `script error` for every settlement line except `event_completion`, the only branch that supplies an `opportunity_id`; because it was uncaught it **aborted the whole settlement transaction**, so placement money, field bonuses and clean-driving bounties were not mis-recorded, they were **never paid**. (c) **four statements in the new `server/graph.lua`**, which I wrote the same day with the same mistake. | NCM; (a) since migration 036, (b) since Economy v1 (`L169`), (c) same-day | none needed now | **FIXED AND DEPLOYED ONLINE 2026-09-28 03:05 UTC.** Every nullable parameter now carries a sentinel and `NULLIF` converts it back: `NULLIF(?, '')` with `""` for strings -- the pattern `market_knowledge.lua` already established -- and `NULLIF(?, 0)` with `0` for nullable BIGINT timestamps, since 0 is never a valid unix time here. No `parameter_count_mismatch` line appears after the restart. **Two lessons, and the second is the real one.** (a) The exposure bug survived for days because of the *shape of the code around it*: the failure was caught, logged at INF among thousands of lines, and `memory[]` held the value for the rest of the process -- so pacing behaved correctly until a restart, then forgot everything, and `once`/`until_ack` silently degraded to `always`. That is `KB-155`'s lesson again: a guard that cannot report having fired is indistinguishable from a bug. (b) **My host harness passed while the code was broken, because the double was more forgiving than the thing it stood in for.** `tools/test-quest-graph.lua` took `values` on trust. It now models the bridge -- counts placeholders, counts `#values`, raises on a mismatch, and flags a nil at any position -- and it carries a deliberate self-check that proves the check can still fail. A double that accepts what production rejects converts a deployable bug into a passing test, which is worse than having no double at all. |

The observed -0.80 race becomes **-0.147**, recovered by one clean 6 km race. `ctx.distanceM` is supplied by `ncm_league/server/progression.lua` from `rec.course.lengthM` (laps included), falling back to the event route. | KB-177 | 2026-09-28 | **Every car in the world silently reverted to its stock gearbox and stock top speed, because the machine channel's port walk was rules-bound to skip the only port the Carrier was on.** Owner report: *"something broke our top speed ceiling and probably physics application on cars, maimai back to stock 103kph"* — the MaiMai is authored at **170 kph** in `data.vehicle_catalogue`, and 103 is the figure the game ships. The Carrier is a **serial** accept loop (`bridge/server.cpp`: `serveWebSocket` blocks until that socket closes), so a port is a lock and not a channel and only one of the client's two hidden surfaces can hold it. `client/carrier_bridge.lua` resolved that with a skip pointed at the wrong channel: the machine walk started at index 1 and refused the FFB channel's port, to stop it displacing a wheel companion mid-corner. **Top speed therefore only ever worked by accident** — the retired companion held 38480, the Carrier fell to 38481, and the machine walk could reach it there. Once the companion stopped being started, the Carrier bound 38480, the machine walk skipped it by rule, and nothing reached any car: no gear headroom, no projection, no governor. The seven carrier logs say it without ambiguity, all on the same plugin build 0.1.28: Carrier on **38481** → 42–126 `gear chain v1` lines per session; Carrier on **38480** → `NCM connected to the bridge` and then **zero**. No log line anywhere named a fault, because from the client's side refusing a port is normal behaviour. | NCM (`ncm_world/client/carrier_bridge.lua`) | start a process holding 38480 before the game so the Carrier falls to 38481 | **FIXED 2026-09-28, NOT YET DEPLOYED.** The skip is inverted: the machine walk sweeps `38480-38483` with no exception, and the FFB walk (`nextFfbUrl`) searches the Rig Bridge's `38520-38523` first, entering the legacy companion range only once `peerIsCarrier` is set and then stepping over the Carrier's own port. Deferring the fallback until the Carrier is found removes the start-up race in which both walks begin on 38480. The companion stays reachable until `§BU` retires it. Gate green: `luac -p` over 255 resource files, 219 panel assertions, docs authority OK. **Live proof is owed: `§BV1` — cold-launch with nothing on 38480, confirm `MACHINE CHANNEL BOUND` and 42 `gear chain v1` lines in the carrier log, then call a FRESH MaiMai and read 170 kph rather than 103.** |
| KB-178 | 2026-09-28 | **The Street Call form had never rendered once, and the fix that shipped for it bounded the wrong hop.** Owner report: *"f5 panel still broken you said you fixed it."* Symptom: F5 does nothing visible. **CORRECTED 2026-09-28 against the actual client log:** the live log carries `no form arrived within 3000 ms (server refused, or the bag did not replicate)`, **not** `FORM DID NOT DRAW` — because `52.60.225.137` was still running the **pre-`L189` state-bag build** (`/opt/ncm/resources/ncm/ncm_world/client/race_builder.lua`, and `web/build.html` still present), so the live failure at that moment was still `KB-172`. Neither `L189` nor this fix had ever been deployed; see `KB-179`. The defect below is real, was read out of the code, and would have been the **next** failure the first time `L189` reached a client — it has never yet had the chance to fail live. **There are two hops and only one was bounded.** `KB-172`/`L189` correctly moved the server→client half off a 4,032-byte player state bag onto `NcmStream`, and that half works. But the client then called `Builder.schemaFromWire(wire)`, which re-expands the streamed node list into **both** `start` (HERE + 35 rows) **and** `finish` (35 rows) — restoring the full **11,515 bytes** — and handed it to the page in a single `page:send("build:open", payload)`. That is `KB-63` exactly: `page:send` drops large messages with no error, no return value and no log line (~16.8 KB proven to vanish while a few dozen bytes drew; `client/panel.lua` splits above four rows for this reason). So the page never received the message, never rendered, never replied `build:drawn`, and the draw watchdog closed the card. The schema was reassembled at the last possible moment before the one hop that could not carry it. **A second, independent failure mode sat underneath:** with `web/build.html`, `ncm_world` held **8 of 8** `kMaximumWebSurfaces` (`IDEA-570`) while `carrier_bridge.lua` and `race.lua`'s bridge probe create pages transiently — so `WebUI.create` could be refused outright depending on timing. **Two things made it invisible:** `venue.html`/`build.html` had no `guard()` and no render harness at all, unlike `panel.html` (KB-61) and `race.html` (L159); and the previous write-up asserted the earlier six-page version *"never worked once"*, which was false — the owner had seen it render, and it rendered precisely because each step sent ≤5 options, a few hundred bytes, under the threshold. **The page count was never the fault.** | NCM (`L189` counted one hop where there are two; and a per-payload size story was accepted over an owner observation that contradicted it) | none; `/ncm.call` and `/ncm.build` failed identically, being the same code path | **FIXED 2026-09-28, OFFLINE-GREEN, LIVE PASS OWED (`§BN1`, `§BN33`-`§BN41`).** Bounded **by construction** rather than by a bigger batch (`L193`): the catalogue never crosses the bridge. `client/race_builder.lua` holds the node list, the form, the cursor and the picker, and sends only what is on screen — at most fourteen form rows or a nine-row picker window, as display strings, with the route length computed in Lua so node coordinates never cross at all. No message's size depends on how many nodes exist, so there is no size at which this can silently stop drawing again. The form is now a block on `web/venue.html` shared through `NcmVenueSurface` and `web/build.html` is **deleted**, putting the resource back to 7 of 8. Because that page is `pointer-events: none` and is never focused (which is what makes it safe during a race, `KB-67`), input is read in Lua with `Open77.input.isDown` — arrows, Enter, Backspace, Delete, PageUp/PageDown and the pad — all **physical reads that need no focus**; the keyboard vocabulary was read out of the deployed `Open77.dll` rather than guessed. **`KB-61` is therefore structurally impossible here: there is no focus to hold, so there is nothing to soft-lock.** `L181`'s one-form shape is unchanged — every field visible, submitted once. New harness `tools/venue-render-check.js` in the gate (**53 assertions**) covers the form, the picker window, refusals, malformed payloads and the pre-existing option-list/history/practice tenants, and asserts the page holds no schema and computes no distances so the payload cannot grow back. Gate: ALL GREEN under both interpreters, `luac -p` over 255 resource files, 219 panel / 55 race / 53 venue / 33 nav assertions. |
| KB-179 | 2026-09-28 | **The Street Call fix was never on the server, and two sessions in a row reported it as deployed.** Owner: *"still doesnt work"* — then, asked, *"and yes its deployed."* It was not. `52.60.225.137:11778` was serving `ncm_world/client/race_builder.lua` dated **Sep 28 00:41 UTC** whose timeout line reads `no form arrived within 3000 ms (server refused, or the bag did not replicate)` — **a string that exists nowhere in the working tree**, because it is the pre-`L189` state-bag build. `web/build.html` was also still present remotely, and `web/venue.html` was dated 00:41 with no `#build` block. So **neither `L189` (`KB-172`) nor `L193` (`KB-178`) had ever reached a client**, and the live failure the owner kept seeing was the original state-bag fault the whole time. **What made it look deployed:** `web/panel.html` on the host is dated **05:21 UTC** with a `panel.html.bak-052150Z` beside it — a *narrow, per-file* application (the `KB-153` pattern: *"the six runtime files were applied narrowly"*) that carried the Messages/quest-graph work and **not** the two Street Call files. A partial deploy leaves a host that is genuinely newer than it was, so "I deployed" is true and "the fix is live" is false, and nothing distinguishes them from the client. Compounding it: the local Windows dev server reaches the working tree through a **junction** (`resources\ncm -> ncm/online/resources`), so local edits are live with no deploy step at all — but the client's `launcher.log` shows every one of the last five launches going to `OP77_CONNECT=52.60.225.137:11778`, the remote world. Editing felt like deploying because on the dev server it is. | NCM (process; and two write-ups asserted a live state neither had checked) | none — the code was correct in the tree and absent on the host | **DIAGNOSED 2026-09-28, NOT YET DEPLOYED — and the deploy is now the whole remaining action for `KB-172`, `KB-178` and `§BN`.** Three things this owes. **(1)** A deployed-version check that is cheap enough to run every time: the client already logs `ncm_world/client loaded \| generation <n>`, and a **build stamp** in that line (a digest of the resource tree, or just the newest mtime) would make "is the fix live" a one-line grep instead of an SSH session. Nothing today can tell a stale host from a fixed one without reading the remote file. **(2)** `tools/deploy-staging.ps1` syncs the **whole** resource tree, which is the right default but is dangerous while the tree carries several concurrent sessions' unvendored work — so the narrow per-file path keeps getting used, and the narrow path is exactly what dropped these two files. A deploy should either be whole-tree or produce a **manifest of what it actually replaced**. **(3)** `KB-115`'s lesson generalises: work that exists in no commit is at risk, and work that exists in no *deploy* is indistinguishable from broken. Recorded as `IDEA-676`. |
| KB-180 | 2026-09-28 | **Player health never regenerated in NCM Online, so every hit a driver took was permanent until they died or disconnected.** Owner: *"player health doesnt regenerate in ncm online, so accrued damage forces a player to eventually leave or die."* Health on OPEN//77 is a **server damage ledger** in absolute points out of `maxHealth` (100), not the engine's pool, and its passive regeneration is a per-player rate that stays at zero until a resource calls `Open77.players.setRegen`. The official freeroam gamemode is what normally sets it (and even it ships `regenPerSecond = 0.0`); `server.jsonc` loads `ncm/*` plus system resources and **not** freeroam, and no NCM file ever called `setRegen`, `setRegenEnabled` or `restoreHealth`. The permission it needs, `players.stats.apply`, was not in any NCM manifest either. Traced from the server's embedded Lua prelude (`Open77.Server.Scripting.dll`), freeroam's `server/combat.lua` and `shared/config.lua`, and `open77_admin/server/players.lua`'s units note. | `ncm_world` (missing service); every player, every session | none in game; an admin `heal` restores the pool once | **FIXED LOCALLY 2026-09-28, NOT DEPLOYED.** `ncm_world/server/vitals.lua` sets `playerRegenPerSecond` (new live tunable, default **2.0 hp/s**, so a full pool in ~50 s; `0` restores the old behaviour) on connect, on resource start for adopted players, and on a 20 s sweep, because nothing documents whether a respawn or revive resets the rate. freeroam's PvP module re-applies it after every placement, which suggests its authors didn't trust it to survive either. `players.stats.apply` added to the manifest. A refusal is logged once, by reason. **Proof owed at `§BV1`-`§BV3`.** The platform constant `DefaultRegenPerSecond` was not read out of the binary; that it is zero comes from the owner's observation plus freeroam's own default. |
| KB-181 | 2026-09-28 | **The first live NCM quick menu (radial 0.1.0) opened but never closed, showed eight blank cards, never showed a highlight, and the mouse still turned the camera.** Owner screenshot + *"radial wheel doesnt go away ever, doesnt list options by name, and i cant see which is highlighted or use mouse to select since mouse still moves camera"*; also *"a bit ugly"*. The host itself worked: native render inside `RadialWheelController`, blur, plugin log `host=RadialWheelController drawn=1`, `resource connected`. **Three causes.** (1) The redscript tick switched to `DelayCallbackNextFrame` when the wheel opened, and that chain never fired again, so the view froze on its first frame: stuck open and no highlight, whatever Lua sent. (2) Open slots put empty fields on the wire (`0|||`); `StrSplit` does not keep empty fields, the count came up short, and `ApplyLayout` refused every layout silently, so no label, title or hint was ever drawn. (3) Nothing held the mouse: `isDown` and the axes are physical reads, and the game kept consuming the mouse for the camera. Also measured: `ncm_radial` was the costliest client resource (226-328 us/frame), mostly from re-resolving `keyFor` and a KVP every tick. | `ncm-radial` 0.1.0, `ncm_radial` resource | Escape or rejoin; every hotkey still works | **FIXED IN CODE, NOT DEPLOYED (radial 0.1.1, resource 0.1.1).** (1) the tick is always `DelayCallback` (0.01 s open, 0.05 s closed). (2) every wire field is non-empty (`~` = empty, stripped from labels); a refused layout now writes `reds: layout refused: N fields` to the plugin log through a new `NcmRadial_Log` native; a state test asserts no `||` can be produced. (3) opened from the keyboard, the wheel takes cursor and keyboard focus on its transport page and freezes rotation (`players.freezeRotation`, `allowAim/Shoot/Interaction false`), the same recipe as the platform's context menu, and aims by cursor travel; every release is Lua-side (`setFocus(false,false)`, `hide`, `resetControls`), Escape closes. Keys are cached (2 s). Visuals redone: open slots are a faint dot, not a card; live slots are framed cards; the selected card is solid accent with dark text; diamond hub. Permissions added: `players.controls`, `local.events`. Live proof still owed: `MASTER_TEST §BX`. |
| KB-182 | 2026-09-28 | **`ncm-vision` 0.2.0 stopped Cyberpunk from starting for every player who joined Online.** RED4ext: *"The following scripts contain invalid native definitions and will prevent your game from starting: ...\\r6\\scripts\\ncm_vision\\NcmVision.reds"*; its log: `Missing native global function 'NcmVision.NcmScanner_Reticle'` (and `_Report`, `_Log`). The three `native func` declarations sat inside `module NcmVision`, so redscript qualified them as `NcmVision.NcmScanner_*`. The DLL loaded and registered them as the globals `NcmScanner_*` (its log says `redscript natives registered`). The names never matched, and RED4ext closes the game before it starts. **The build's safety check could not see it:** `scc` compiles a native inside a module without complaint; only RED4ext's validation at game start knows what was registered. The Radial works because its natives are declared with no module. | NCM Scanner (`ncm-vision` 0.2.0) | none for players (the game will not start); a player whose launch failed is repaired by the next Connect | **FIXED AND DEPLOYED 2026-09-28 17:34 UTC as `ncm-vision` 0.2.1** (sha256 `73ce73ae...`, digest `46cfc058...`; 0.2.0 was live about 5 minutes). The natives moved to `NcmScannerNatives.reds`, which has no module. `tools/build-vision.ps1` now refuses any `.reds` that declares natives inside a module, and any native the DLL's `rtti.cpp` does not register. The guard was checked against 0.2.0's shape. The deployed 0.2.1 scripts cite this bug as `KB-181` in two comments, because that id was taken concurrently; the source now says `KB-182`, and the difference is comment-only. **Lesson:** a redscript native's registered name is part of the contract, and a compile check is not a load check. |

Nav now keys focus (`data-nav-key`, id, tab id, label, then position) and moves it onto the replacement node before acting, and a `MutationObserver` does the same on redraw. Enter/CONFIRM cycles a dropdown (a native popup is not guaranteed to draw off-screen). Left/Right belong to a focused slider, dropdown or text field. Dropdowns release on blur. | KB-187 | 2026-09-28 | **Rig Bridge created all four effects, but its two streamed effects could remain silent while SPRING and DAMPER worked.** SINE changed `DIPERIODIC::dwPeriod` with revs and used `DIEP_NORESTART`; on `DIERR_EFFECTPLAYING` it fell back to `DIEP_START`. A driver that cannot change the periodic parameters live therefore restarted the waveform repeatedly from phase zero. CONSTANT used the same helper. Worse, both writers updated `sentSine_` / `sentConstant_` before checking `SetParameters`, so any refusal other than that one special case was remembered as success and might never be retried. The earlier `KB-184` diagnosis was wrong: `ncm_rig_bridge.user.json` was written from the startup `Config`, while the live setters changed only atomics, so its `strength 0.020` and `armed false` did not show what the running bridge had received. The slider fix remains valid UI work; it was not evidence for the missing rumble. | NCM Rig Bridge 0.1.1 DirectInput output | external companion remains the proved fallback | **FIXED IN RIG BRIDGE 0.1.2; LIVE PASS OWED (`§CC`).** SINE uses a fixed 125 Hz carrier whose magnitude is updated at no more than 50 Hz, allowing complete cycles even on a restart-only driver. Updates omit both `DIEP_START` and `DIEP_NORESTART`, so DirectInput preserves playback when possible and performs the driver-required compatibility restart otherwise. A failed write is not cached; acquisition is retried once. Effects are no longer started/stopped at 250 Hz while armed outside a current vehicle frame. Persistent FORCE/class/tune values now copy into `Config` before save; ARM remains session-only by safety rule. Package SHA-256 `ee818938cdc0e1aa8152b69f21719f993dd6adbefbf49de2354dc2cb7c9bffc5`. |

| KB-209 | 2026-09-29 | **The G29's force feedback died on every ARM until the wheel was replugged: Rig Bridge 0.1.6's SDL3 had opened it.** Owner: *"my wheel ffb disconnects every time i arm ffb, i have to unplug and replug in the wheel, at which point it works."* Rig Bridge log 11:01:46: `SDL3 joystick joystick:Logitech G29 Driving Force Racing Wheel (25 buttons)` and `joystick:Thrustmaster Sim Pedals`. At 01:17 the same session showed `FFB LOST ... (0x80040205)`, then the G29 dropping out of DirectInput and SDL re-opening it 2 s after it came back. SDL 3.4 carries a HIDAPI Logitech wheel driver (lg4ff) that sends the wheel its own commands when it opens it. The gamepad layer's raw-joystick discovery opened *every* non-gamepad device, wheel included. It is the Carrier's SDL-haptic theft (`L201`) in a new form: two owners of one force-feedback device. | NCM (mine: `IDEA-705` gamepad layer) | replug the wheel | **FIXED IN RIG BRIDGE 0.1.7, DEPLOYED 2026-09-29 15:18 UTC; LIVE PROOF OWED.** SDL never touches a device DirectInput reads. `SDL_JOYSTICK_HIDAPI_LG4FF=0`. `SDL_JOYSTICK_IGNORE_DEVICES` is built from DirectInput's own device list (seeded before SDL starts and refreshed on hot-plug). Nintendo, Sony and Microsoft are exempt: they are the motion hands. Raw joysticks of type wheel, throttle or flight stick are skipped, and anything DirectInput later claims is released. Those devices still reach the gamepad layer as `di:<VID:PID>`. Live proof: `§CG1` (no `SDL3 joystick` line for G29/pedals) and `§CD`-style ARM with no replug. |

| KB-211 | 2026-09-29 | **The Scanner read "READING…" forever on AI traffic, and later on the owner's own Galena; chrome could not be uploaded onto traffic.** The server log held 93 `ncm_league script error: Open77 instruction quota exceeded` lines from 2026-09-28 17:40 (eleven minutes after `ncm_scanner` first started), in every play session, and exactly one `scan query` line ever. **Cause, two parts.** (1) A "vanilla" query built the record index inside the handler with a portable CRC32 that XORed bit by bit: ~3.3M VM instructions over 100 records. It blew the quota, the handler died before replying, the index was never kept, and every retry died the same way. (2) A car went up as "vanilla" whenever the client's `aimedEntity` match missed (on foot beside a car, a moving car), which included players' cars and NCM traffic; with no vehicle id the client could not start chrome on them either. | NCM Scanner (`ncm_league` collection, `ncm_scanner` client) | none | **FIXED AND DEPLOYED 2026-09-29 15:45 UTC** with `ncm-vision` 0.3.0 (sha256 `e44ecafa...`, digest `879af328...`). XOR is native `~` where the interpreter has it, else a nibble table (170k / 1.25M instructions for the whole index); the index is built in yielding slices of 8 at resource start (`scanner record index ready: 100 records` at boot) and queries before then are answered busy. The DLL now reports the looked-at point's world position; the server finds the network vehicle within 6 m of it and returns its id and operator, so traffic and players' cars resolve without `aimedEntity`. `tools/test-scanner-host.lua` covers both (19 checks). **Lesson:** OPEN//77 meters every handler; anything O(catalogue) belongs in a yielding start-up thread, never on a request path. **Live proof owed:** scan and hack NCM traffic. |

| KB-217 | 2026-09-29 | **Night City shows bright sun at 21:30 Eastern: the real-time Eastern clock (`IDEA-729`) was never built, and it cannot be built from the server.** The Paddock schedules on Eastern time internally (`world.clock`), but nothing sets the game clock, so `open77_weather` runs its default 8x day from noon. OPEN//77's own weather guide rules out the server route: its clock projection writes the game clock only past a 120-game-second drift, and **every write is a world time-jump** that re-resolves world nodes and can bring back destroyed props. The engine always runs at 8x, so any other server rate (real time, or frozen) means a jump every ~15-17 s, forever. Scripts expose no rate (only `SetGameTimeByHMS` jumps and time dilation, which slows everything). |

| KB-238 | 2026-09-30 | **Crash to desktop while spectating a live Paddock race (dev, `pr-regional_c-1790800200`, 12 cars, Pace backend).** Owner: *"ctd after a decent start. field was kind of separating ... maybe [something] got destreamed"*. Same signature as three earlier crashes today (02:46, 04:58, 06:30): `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` reading `0xC0` at `Cyberpunk2077.exe+0x2543394` (a null object's field), game code on the main thread, no Open77 or NCM frame among the captured stack words. In **all four** OPEN//77 crash-probe records the newest breadcrumb, 172-828 ms before the fault, is `vphys entity=... obs=0 keepBody=0` (a vehicle switched to unobserved physics with its body dropped). This time: green 16:30:10; `vdespawn` of six entities 10-17 s before the fault; a fresh `Dynamic entity service spawned` and repeated AI-driver re-mounts in the last two seconds; car `0x7D00000003` to `obs=0` then `VAUDIO detach` for the same car 208 ms before the crash. Cars were running from about (300, 1260) toward (-1106, 1853), 1.5 km away, so the spectator's stream range is the likely trigger. Server side quiet: no NCM bubble hand-off logged. The Pace log for pid 37436 stops at 16:30:41 (either Pace stopped commanding streamed-out cars, or buffered lines were lost in the crash). | Platform (vehicle observation/physics) likely; NCM Pace involvement unproven | do not spectate a live race from the start line as the field leaves stream range | **OPEN, ROOT CAUSE UNPROVEN.** Five crashes share this signature; the crash dump reports a failed `vehicleCarBaseObject` to `gamePuppet` dynamic cast in game code. One direct-route control race survived observation and ownership changes, so those alone do not isolate the trigger. Next evidence, not fixes: (1) whether any crash with this signature happened without a live Pace race; (2) one spectate with `liveBackend` = followRoute to split Pace from the platform; (3) report the signature and breadcrumbs to the OPEN//77 devs, who own `vphys`. **UPDATE 2026-10-01 (00:43, 00:46, 00:54; Pace 0.8.0):** three more, identical (`Cyberpunk2077.exe+0x2543394`, read at `0xC0`, main thread, newest breadcrumb `vphys ... obs=0 keepBody=0` 219-250 ms before). **Question (1) answered: the 00:43:53 crash happened with Pace doing NOTHING** -- zero racing commands, no drive plan, no traffic area in that session's Pace log -- so the crash needs neither Pace nor a race. It follows Open77 moving vehicles in and out of observed physics. Eight crashes now share the signature (2026-09-30 02:46, 04:58, 06:30, 16:31, 17:00; 2026-10-01 00:43, 00:46, 00:54). **CORRECTION 01:10 (owner: "would rather look in the mirror"):** the breadcrumb entities ARE ours. 00:46 and 00:54: the vehicles Open77 switched to `obs=0 keepBody=0` just before the fault are Paddock race cars (TRACE entity ids; slot 11 logged `DROPOUT` 3 s before). 00:43 (no race): an AI car with an NPC driver being seated, spawned 1.7 s earlier -- the `ncm_world` ambient pattern (`ambient.lua` seats NPC drivers). Common factor: an NCM AI car with an NPC seated by `Open77.vehicles.ai.attachDriver` (AI suspended) leaving observation; seated drivers began with L234 the evening before the first crash. No NCM module on any stack, so the trigger is our setup, not our code at the fault. NB the driver/driverless results of 2026-09-30 23:36-23:54 are VOID (broken hash, `KB-249`). Next: driverless races (`livePaceDrivers` off) followed until the field spreads. **FIX BUILT 2026-10-01 01:30 (`L247`), dev; awaiting live PASS (`MASTER_TEST §CN`).** Owner: "a driverless car is not a solution, come back with a solution that keeps drivers in cars". So no car with a seated driver is kept alive out at the edge of a client's streaming: (1) a street race is no longer a grid run (twelve driven cars alive however far from the host they got); it runs on the continuous model and the physical bubble, on the baked road line (`IDEA-787`): pre-grid at the start 30 s before the lights, a car goes back to the model once its Pace player is past `bubbleOutM` (300 m; the driver is removed before the car), and comes back in, driver seated, within `bubbleInM` (250 m) of a Pace player, as often as that happens (`IDEA-786`); (2) `ncm_world` ambient cars retire past `ambientRetireM` (now 300 m, was 420) whether or not a client still streams them; (3) Pace 0.8.1 + the client hand a re-created car's new entity to Pace (`car.entity`) instead of driving nothing. Harness 332/332 (both Luas), Pace state tests pass. Status stays OPEN until a session of races with no CTD. **UPDATE 2026-10-01 14:10 -- ninth crash (02:06:34, first street race on the bubble, Pace 0.8.1), and the sequence, from the client log against the probe breadcrumbs:** bubble car vehicle `12884901902` spawned 02:06:32.761 (`cyberEntity 0x8C00000025`), engine physics OBSERVED 33.742, driver seat mounted 33.920, in the workspot 34.028, physics UNOBSERVED 34.351, fault 34.664. 00:46 the same (spawned 48.31, observed 49.30, mounted ~49.6, unobserved 50.18, fault 51.05); 00:54 the same on a re-created car (observed 38.39, mounted 38.607, fault 38.638). Every new car -- crashing or not -- goes through the engine's own physics cycle in the client's game: observed ~1.0 s after it appears, unobserved ~0.5 s later (pre-grid car `4294967314`: 32.32 / 33.31 / 33.82); the NPC's seat mount lands 1.1-1.7 s in, INSIDE that cycle, and most survive -- so every driven spawn was a gamble. `vphys` is Open77's hook on that engine switch (`vphys entity=%016llX obs=%d keepBody=%d` in Open77.dll); the platform refuses authority over AI-driven cars (`ai_driven`, L229), so the switch cannot be held. **Mitigation built (dev, L247 addendum 14:10): no seat mount inside the spawn cycle.** A bubble Pace car comes up EMPTY; the host's client reports it once the car has stood in its game for `bubbleSeatDelayMs` (3 s, restarting if re-created); only then is the driver seated and Pace given the car; never settled in `bubbleSeatTimeoutMs` (20 s) = removed. `ncm_world` ambient cars likewise: the driver is seated once a client has held the car for `ambientSeatDelayMs` (3 s). Harness 339/339, ambient 45/45, gate all green. Still to report to OPEN//77 with this sequence: the fault itself is in the engine's handling of a platform-seated NPC. **UPDATE 14:25 (owner: "how close to the observable line of streaming are we?"):** unknown -- L229 put the client's pickup only somewhere between 186 m and 573 m, and the bubble's 250 m in / 300 m out sits inside that band; the server's distance logs went to its console and were lost. Instrumented (dev, no behaviour change): the Pace player's client reports every change in a race car's state (streamed, engine entity, physics owner); the server measures the distance and writes it back to that client's own log as `STREAMLINE ...`, with every set-down and hand-back distance. Next: one race session, then set the bubble's radii a safe margin inside the measured line. |

If they MATCH and commands still fail, the cause is elsewhere (the racer's own requirements) -- next step then. **UPDATE 23:36 (Pace 0.7.6, 20 s test window):** the engine keeps sector nodes in packed content, so the hash could not be read; instead a CONTROL: `AIVehicleOnSplineCommand` on a VANILLA open-world spline (`#test_nc_sq024_city_center`, exterior_-1_-1_0_6) by its path through Pace's hash went to `Enqueued` and STAYED Enqueued for 20 s; the same command on OUR spline never left `NotExecuting`. So (1) Pace's hash resolves a vanilla node -- our nodes are what the engine does not register (AlwaysLoaded and Exterior-6 sectors both, ArchiveXL merged the block each time); (2) even a resolvable spline command is never executed on these cars -- leading suspect: spline/racing commands run in the DRIVER's behaviour tree and the seated drivers are Open77 NPCs in `Open77.npcs.ai.tasks` mode, not vanilla AI (drive-to-point, which is vehicle-autonomous, did run). Next: a driverless grid (`livePaceDrivers` false), then a vanilla-AI driver if needed. **UPDATE 23:54 (driverless):** the vanilla spline command FAILED in 1 tick with nobody in the seat (vs Enqueued forever with an Open77 task-AI driver): spline/racing commands need a driver the GAME's AI runs. **Built (dev, not committed -- shared files):** Paddock street drivers are created with `Open77.npcs.ai.native` (the mode the platform's PvP bots use for vanilla behaviour); `livePaceDrivers` back to true; harness 332/332. Our own spline nodes still not registering is the second, separate problem. **CORRECTION 00:05 (evidence, owner challenge):** the native-AI change was unfounded and is REVERTED. `vehicle/default_vehicle.behavior` (extracted) runs AIVehicleRacingCommand in the VEHICLE's own tree, in the same command selector as drive-to-point, chase, patrol and panic, with no driver condition; `AIDriveCommandsDelegate.DoStartDriveRacing` only stores the command. Open77's vehicle-AI guide: `attachDriver` with an NPC suspends that NPC's AI, so its AI mode cannot matter. The vanilla control spline sits in a variant-bearing sector and may not have been loaded, so the driver/driverless results do not discriminate. Remaining blocker: our spline is not found. Next test: visible objects baked into our own sector, to prove whether ArchiveXL instantiates our sectors at all. **FOUND 00:05 (WolvenKit diff vs vanilla exterior_-2_0_0_4, a working open-world vehicle spline):** every one of 32,106 vanilla Exterior nodes has a nonzero 42-bit global node `Id` (cook-allocated, 0x248d0368450-0x24ac6e21f40 near Heywood); ours were 0 (copied from quest/always-loaded templates, where all nodes are 0). **Pace 0.7.7:** unique Ids above the vanilla range (0x3F000000000 + n*16), `UkFloat1` = leg length. Live result owed. **ROOT CAUSE 00:36 (Codeware diag, dev-only):** Pace's NodeRef hash was wrong. The engine's NodeRef = FNV-1a 64 of the node path with every `#` removed (Codeware `CreateNodeRef` == the ref the engine holds in a loaded sector, for our spline 0xe9819f95dc072143 and vanilla's cleaning-machine spline 0x0a13e2f0fdb27076; neither matches FNV-1a of the full path). Our sectors and nodes were registered correctly throughout; every racing command named a non-existent spline. **FIXED in Pace 0.8.0** (both pairs are tests). The intermediate fixes (always-loaded/exterior sectors, node flags, global node ids, native-AI drivers) were not the cause. **Id range checked 2026-10-01:** a scan of every world sector's node Ids (`tools/scan-node-ids.py`, base + EP1, 9,790,527 distinct, highest `0x24acab7ff20`) found none in the bake's range `0x3f000000000`-`0x3f000004a70`. |

### KB-238 recurrence evidence

2026-10-01 15:46 CTD: KB-238 same +0x2543394/read 0xC0 on Pace 0.8.2. Racer p00786 spawned at 87 m; settled after 0 ms; mounted 1.171 s after spawn; fault 684 ms after mount. Latest vphys is obs=1, not obs=0; last despawn 24 s earlier. Close bubble did not prevent recurrence. Spawn/mount overlap remains a hypothesis; exact faulting object unproven. Full evidence: ../research/open77/PACE_ROAD_PATH_PROBE.md, 15:46 addendum. Diagnosis only; no runtime changes.

### KB-238 native trace follow-up

2026-10-01: minidump plus exe disassembly identifies input vehicleCarBaseObject entity 0x9F4D8D (p00786) failing a gamePuppet cast. Caller passes the null handle to +0x2543388, faulting at +0x2543394. Associated moveComponent belongs to its driver 0x9F4D8E. Immediate mechanism proven; reason for wrong-type input still open. Paddock simulation math not implicated by code trace. Full evidence: ../research/open77/PACE_ROAD_PATH_PROBE.md follow-up.

| KB-328 | 2026-10-03 | **NPC part purchases fail with SQL 1690 every 5 minutes.** `[ERR] [database] resource 'ncm_paddock' transaction statement 4/5 failed ... (SQL 1690)`, at 30 s and then every 300 s (`PaddockMarkets.shop`). The statement is `SET p.cash = p.cash - mi.price` in `partPurchaseTx`: `mi.price` is unsigned, so MariaDB does the arithmetic unsigned and errors instead of going negative, whenever the NPC's database cash is below the price, i.e. its in-memory affordability disagrees with the database. The whole purchase rolls back: no money or parts are corrupted. First seen at the 23:34 boot (l311: 5; l312: 2); zero in l304-l310. That boot changed only the collapse planner, so the cause is probably data state, not established. | ncm_paddock markets (L298) | none | **FIXED 2026-10-04.** Cause found: `PaddockMarkets.shop` runs `seedCash`, which credits a never-seeded independent's MEMORY (1,500-6,000) and only marks them for the next flush; the purchase SQL then checked the DATABASE, which did not have the seed yet. The pools grew by 47 people, so every pass met new unseeded shoppers (the drift ids advanced each boot: p01338-41, then p01342-44). Fix: `purchasePart` flushes the store first, as the Exchange's settle already did; any difference left is real drift, which is logged and resolved in the database's favour; the unsigned subtraction is `CAST(mi.price AS SIGNED)` in both part and car purchases. Exchange settlement now reads both NPCs' cash back from the database after the commit and yields around it (KB-323's quota cut-off could leave memory ahead). Boot `l316-restart-20261004.stdout.log`: purchases complete, no SQL 1690, no drift lines. **Dev, 02:08-06:00: 61 purchases, 0 SQL 1690, 0 drift, 1 settlement, 0 failures. Online still has it (56 SQL 1690 in 24 h).** A minimal hotfix of only these hunks against `online-baseline-2` is built and syntax-checked (L305 item 6), and **awaits the owner's yes**. |
